## Weights of evidence

**R06. The model's step takes the first verdict of each item, while help — a rung of the ladder before it, a technique on request, a sketch as a hint, a solution already seen — only keeps "mastered" from being set. Each of the N answers of one attempt weighs 1/(1 + (N − 1)/3), one piece of evidence gives one step, and "mastered" is decided by pieces of evidence. The item's primary skills share the step, after the transfer by `retired_skills`, 1/J each without a major version, the supporting ones get nothing. A key from the author, from a solver or from an adult with tags not from AI weighs 1, any other piece of evidence — 0, and moves nothing.** The model is in [architecture.md](architecture.md), its sources in [sources.md](sources.md).
- **What is decided:**
  - an item's answer is its first verdict in the attempt, "correct" or "not yet". Later answers of the same item do not go into the step, and the number of the hint k does not change the step. An item first answered after a rung goes with help: its number of wrong answers before the submission that gave the first verdict is greater than zero;
  - help is a rung of the ladder before the item's first verdict, a technique on request anywhere in the attempt, the outcome "with a sketch as a hint", which is help for all the answers of the attempt, and a task's solution that has already gone to the device — that is, almost any repeat of a solved task, not of a template. An answer with help goes into the step, into the counters and into the stability as it is, but does not set "mastered". A technique at a step is not help;
  - a series is all the items of one attempt: each of the N answers weighs u = 1/(1 + (N − 1) · ρ), ρ = 1/3. For θ and n, N is the evidence's items with an answer; for a skill's δ and m, the items with an answer where it is primary, after the transfer. The format needs no mark of items being of one type, and a task of one item weighs 1;
  - one piece of evidence is one step: every P comes from the state before it, and the shifts add up. K_θ and K_δ go by n and m before the piece of evidence, and v after it. n, m, the five answers of the trial series and the five answers before "mastered" are sums of the weights u. The trial series is whole pieces of evidence of the module while n < 5; its likelihood is weighted, and the module's state stores u for each of its answers;
  - "mastered" goes by pieces of evidence. A piece of evidence fails a skill if the skill's correct answers in it are fewer than half; two failures in a row remove "mastered", and a piece of evidence without a failure sets it if it has a correct answer without help and m ≥ 5. Removing is checked before setting, and one piece of evidence does not do both;
  - a failure of the module, which the guard after three failures in a row counts, is not a skill's failure: it is a piece of evidence past the trial series on which the model expected the learner to know at least half of the answers, above the guessing floor, and fewer than half of the items with an answer were solved at the end of the attempt. The step and a skill's failure stay as above;
  - the transfer by `retired_skills` goes inside the step. Each recorded primary skill x of an item goes to the skills map(x): to itself, if it is in `skills` of the current version with the assessability `verdict`; to its `replaced_by` with `verdict`, if it is retired; to none in all other cases. J is the number of the item's recorded primary skills whose map(x) is not empty — without a major version, all of them —, and a skill s gets in an item the share |{x : s ∈ map(x)}| / J of the step of δ: 1/J without a major version, the former skill's share in full for each part of a split skill, the sum of the shares for a merged skill. m grows by the whole u;
  - N_s, u_s, m, the stability, the time of the last practice and the number of pieces of evidence are counted by the skills after the transfer, once per item: the skills merged in one item are one skill, and their count and practice are not doubled. An item's δ̄ is the mean over its J recorded skills, and each recorded skill's δ is the mean of the δ of its skills after the transfer; R̄ likewise. For an item without skills after the transfer, J = 0, δ̄ = 0, R̄ = 1 and w = 1: it moves only θ and n. J and δ̄ go by the skill IDs recorded in the piece of evidence, and the sums in a canonical order: items by place, skills by ID;
  - supporting skills get nothing — no step, no counters, no stability;
  - outcomes go by items. At "solution shown", an item without a first verdict that was not submitted, or whose submissions are all "unreadable" with the reason `blank`, is "not solved", S = 0; an item with another "unreadable", with "unchecked", or submitted without a verdict and without reasons gives no answer: it is not an error. At "left unfinished" only the items with a verdict go; "unchecked" weighs nothing by itself, and the verdicts of the items before it go. Practice belongs to the skills that have an answer in the piece of evidence;
  - the stability of a skill whose answers are not all correct changes by the share of correct ones S̄: τ := max(τ₀; τ · (1 + g · (1 − R))^S̄ · (1/2)^(1 − S̄));
  - provenance: a piece of evidence weighs 1 only when its key is from the author, from a solver or entered by an adult and its skill tags are from the author, a solver or an adult. A key or tags derived by AI, a provenance "not specified" and one this version does not know weigh 0. A piece of evidence at weight 0 moves nothing, like one without answers: neither θ and δ, nor n and m, nor practice and τ, nor traps, nor "mastered", and it creates no state for the module. An adult's key also needs a band and a difficulty, which `Append` requires of any piece of evidence;
  - a trap that the program found weighs 1 in the count of traps and goes to the skills of its `skills` in the catalogue that have a state after the step, and without them, to the module. For each trap a skill has the number of pieces of evidence where the program found it, once per piece of evidence, and the time of the last one; a trap with skills without a state is counted at the module, as one without `skills` is. A trap that AI supposed weighs 0: the piece of evidence does not carry it. Which traps are frequent, the readers decide;
  - a probe counts as any task, without a lift;
  - "explain differently" and a task being a segment of a lesson do not change the weights, and there is no place for the weight of feedback by rubric.
- **Decided apart:** what a piece of evidence carries of first verdicts, help, provenance and traps, and which facts without weight it keeps for future versions — the evidence and the log; the types; knowing an anchor skill. Which traps are frequent, when to take a skill off pause and after how long to give a task again is `engine`'s.

— *Why:*
- **The first verdict.** The guessing floor c and P describe the first try: after a wrong answer and a hint the probability is different, and there are no data on what it is. So it is in standalone too, "The answer is recorded once" (sources 1.8), and KDD Cup 2010 measured models by "Correct First Attempt" — "the tutor's evaluation of the student's first attempt on the step—1 if correct, 0 if an error" (sources 2.5). The log carries how many wrong answers there were before the first verdict, and a new version can read partial credit by recomputation.
- **Help only in "mastered".** In standalone "The hint, the pace and the run of failures are recorded … and change what is chosen next … — never the rating", and "mastered" is set only after an answer without a hint: "The hint would make the answer measure the hint" (sources 1.8). The request and "explain differently" do not change the outcome in `spec`'s rules of the outcome either. A technique at a step is not help: the new profile goes "by steps", and in this mode "mastered" would otherwise never come. A sketch is help for the whole attempt: as after a hint, a sketch at the start of the attempt would give "mastered" from the first submission.
- **A repeat of a task is help, not weight 0.** The solution goes to the device when the attempt is closed by a correct answer or by showing the solution, so "the solution has already been seen" is almost any repeat of a solved task, not of a template, and a workbook page is never a template in format v0. At weight 0 a repetition of a mastered skill on an exhausted pool would not refresh τ: the date would not move, and the engine would give the skill again and again. As help, a repeat refreshes τ but does not set "mastered": the learner could remember the key.
- **The discount by attempt.** "Ten fractions in five minutes are not ten independent pieces of evidence", as `spec`'s vocabulary says. Models that count the answers of one stem as independent "will overestimate the precision with which examinee proficiency is measured", and this leads to "prematurely ending an examination" (sources 2.6) — here, an early "mastered". 1/(1 + (N − 1)ρ) is the share of the effective sample size at an intra-class correlation ρ: the effect of a cluster "depends on two factors: the subsample size within selected PSUs (b) and the intra-class correlation (ρ)" (sources 2.7). At ρ = 1/3 an attempt weighs no more than three answers, and a task of one item exactly one, so the module of olympiad maths counts as before.
- **The discount over the whole attempt, not over a set of skills.** Otherwise items with the skills {A} and {A, B} on one page would get no discount, and a page of four sets of five items would shift θ like 8.6 answers. The answers depend on the shared stem and one session, not on the set of skills.
- **One step per piece of evidence.** The order of a page's items then changes nothing, as the retention of a skill is one per piece of evidence, and what is appended is counted by one function of the piece of evidence, which makes it simpler for the appended state to match the recomputed one.
- **"Mastered" by pieces of evidence.** Two answers "not yet" in a row by the order of places on the page would depend on where the author put the items, and scattered errors would never remove "mastered". For a task of one item a failure by the share of correct answers is exactly a wrong answer, and two failures in a row are standalone's two wrong answers in a row (sources 1.7).
- **The share of the step, and m in full.** P has the mean δ̄ of the item's primary skills, and a shift of δ̄ by an answer's step is 1/J of the step to each of the J skills: 1/J is the derivative of the log-likelihood by a skill's δ, not a quote — the multidimensional extension of Elo keeps each task in one knowledge component (sources 2.8). m counts the answers where the skill is primary: at 1/J, a skill that always stands in a pair would need twice as many tasks for "mastered". For a split skill the derivative by the δ of one of two parts is half as large, and the share in full holds by another reason: the δ of each part goes exactly as the δ of the former skill, and the item's δ̄ does not change with a major version — also when the item has other primary skills.
- **Nothing to the supporting ones.** They are not in P, and an answer tells the model nothing about them, while a step only at a correct answer would pull their estimate up without measure. An error does not lower them in `spec`'s vocabulary either.
- **Outcomes by items.** Showing the solution is "not solved", as `spec` decided, and "I don't know" in standalone is a wrong answer: "a child who has seen the solution has not solved the task" (sources 1.8). "Left unfinished" is not "not solved" for `spec` either, and what the learner did not answer the model does not judge, like a skip in standalone: "punishing that would punish a stray tap" (sources 1.8). "Unchecked" and "unreadable" other than blank are not an error, as no deterministic check means no verdict, but the verdicts before "unchecked" were given by the program, and there is no reason to lose them: `spec` rejected "unchecked" for a left attempt for exactly that reason, that at zero weight "the incorrect answers before leaving would be lost to the model".
- **τ by the share of correct answers.** At S̄ = 1 and 0 these are the rules of forgetting for all correct and none correct, and one miss on a page of ten does not halve τ, as an answer "not yet" would. There are no new constants.
- **Provenance.** With a key derived by AI there is no verdict, and a task from paper that AI itself tagged and solved "does not move the rating", as `spec`'s vocabulary says (О-11). The vocabulary counts an adult's key "when … the skills are not from AI", so tags from a solver or an adult weigh 1 too. An adult's key gives a verdict (О-11), but a task from paper has no band and difficulty on the module's scale, and without them P cannot be computed. A provenance this version does not know may turn out as unreliable as AI, and a field that changes the weight acts only in a version that knows it. Weight 0 moves nothing at all: if it moved the counters and practice, tags from AI would decide whose skill was practised. `spec`'s list of evidence does not put an AI trap into the evidence, so its weight is 0, not "reduced", as the vocabulary writes.
- **A probe without a lift.** Standalone's lift of a probe relied on the pause — a rule of choice —, and standalone recognized a probe by where the learner stood: "Whether a task is a probe is read off where the child stands when it is answered" (sources 1.8). Here the pause is a rule of `engine`, and the model would have to read its state. A correct answer at a low P raises δ strongly anyway.
- **No place for the rubric.** An attempt of a task with a rubric has no evidence (О-17): the weights need no place, and the weight of feedback will come with a new version together with the answer to О-17.

— *Rejected:*
- partial credit by the number of hints, for example S = 1/2^k: new constants without data, and the log carries k anyway — it can be introduced later;
- the outcome as a multiplier of the step at S, the item's result at the end of the attempt: a correct answer after three hints would go as correct, only weaker;
- an answer with help at weight 1/2: one more constant without data; a technique at a step as help too: in the mode "by steps" "mastered" would not come if the module's steps give a technique; a sketch "as after a hint", for the items after it alone: above, "Help only in "mastered"";
- a request for a technique as an error of the first attempt, like a request for a hint in KDD Cup 2010 ("an error (incorrect attempt or hint request)", sources 2.5): a technique names a way, not an answer, and `spec`'s rules of the outcome do not let the request into it;
- a discount of √N — it grows without bound; a series as one answer with the share of correct ones — a page of twenty items would weigh like one task; a discount by an identical set of skills — above, "The discount over the whole attempt";
- a step for each answer in the order of the items, with "not yet" in a row at a total weight of 2: the order of places on the page would start to mean something; counters by pieces of evidence, with weight only in the step: a page of ten and one task would weigh the same for the uncertainty and "mastered";
- the whole step of δ to each primary skill: at J = 2 δ̄ would shift twice as much as one answer; m by 1/J — above;
- τ and the time of practice to the supporting ones at a correct answer: "time to review" would be put off by an answer that says nothing about that skill;
- not reading "unchecked" at all: the verdicts that the program has already given would be lost; showing without "not solved" for empty items: an adult who showed the solution at once would not move the model;
- a repeat of a task at weight 0 and at weight 1/2: above, "A repeat of a task is help";
- τ: all correct — it grows, otherwise by half — one miss out of ten would halve τ; a majority — a jump at the boundary;
- an adult's key at weight 0 until stage 5 — О-11 gives it a verdict; AI traps in the evidence at weight 1/2 — `spec`'s list of evidence does not put them there, and this would become a row to `spec`;
- weight 0 that moves only the rating: tags from AI would then decide whose skill was practised, and a skill would sometimes have practice without m; weight 1 for a provenance "not specified" or unknown: above, "Provenance";
- creating a state for a skill for the sake of a trap: a skill has a state only with practice;
- a lift on a probe by a mark "probe" in the evidence from `engine` — a field that standalone rejected in its profile, and a reachability threshold in the model;
- a place in the weights for feedback by rubric at weight 0 — there is no evidence, and no place is needed.

— *Consequence:* [architecture.md](architecture.md); `CLAUDE.md`; `README.md`; `step.go`, `model.go`, `rules.go` and their tests; `transfer_test.go`; `testdata/fold/`, `testdata/read/`; [run/DEBT.md](../run/DEBT.md).

## Knowing an anchor skill

**R07. The learner knows an anchor skill when "mastered" is set on at least one skill that teaches it — in any module whose graph came, and on any band. "Does not know" is given only by the modules that the engine gets, and otherwise the anchor skill is "unknown". The model gives each state with a reason and the skills that teach it, and computes it at reading, together with two marks of each module's trial series. Forgetting does not remove knowing, and skills with `rubric` and `none` have no state.** The rule is in [architecture.md](architecture.md).
- **What is decided:**
  - knows — when "mastered" is set on at least one skill that teaches the anchor skill, in any module and on any band. The threshold is the one of "mastered", there is no own one; several skills and modules give "or". The model gives the mastery band and the highest band where the skill is taught, and knowing with regard to the band is for later;
  - "knows" goes by "mastered" in any module whose graph came at reading: a module of the log, connected or not, and a connected module without evidence. "Does not know" goes only by the engine's modules, connected and suitable for the learner. For this, reading has one more input — the set of the engine's modules;
  - three states with a reason: "knows"; "does not know" — with the reason "not mastered" if a skill with `verdict` that teaches it in the engine's modules has answers, and "no evidence" if none of them has; "unknown" — with the reason "nobody to teach it", or "only skills that cannot be mastered teach it": without a verdict, or with `verdict` but primary in no band and so without a band where they are taught, as in a module without tasks. The name of the second reason, `AnchorReasonNoVerdictTeacher`, is left from v0.1.0. Whether to hold a lesson back at "no evidence" is decided by `engine`. The grade does not give knowing;
  - with each state the model gives the skills that teach it, by full ID compared byte by byte, with the estimate, "mastered", the bands, the retention, the review date and the flag `Practised`; a teacher without a state has only the ID and the highest band. The mastery band and the highest band are a `BandRef`, with the ID from the graph of the current version, and a module without a current version has an empty ID;
  - forgetting does not remove knowing: knowing is a function of "mastered", and the model gives the retention and the review date of the skill that teaches it together with it;
  - skills with `rubric` and `none` have no state;
  - the anchor skills are each `core.<name>` that the graph of an engine's module with a current version names as a prerequisite or in `teaches`. The skills that teach them are taken from the modules of the state and the engine's modules with a current version: "mastered" exists only in a module with a state, so "any module whose graph came" is read without walking the map "graphs". A module without a current version gives no graph, and its "mastered" is not seen;
  - an anchor skill as a supporting skill of a task gets nothing, and the model does not read it in the evidence. The model does not read the module index;
  - the mark "the module's last piece of evidence was still in the series" is a field of the module's state, set only by a step that moved the module: a piece of evidence at weight 0 or without answers changes nothing. "The series has ended" is not stored: it is n ≥ 5 − ε. `Append` and `Read` reject a state where the module is still in the series and the mark is not set: the step does not write such a state;
  - `Read` takes the state, the graphs, t₀, the grade and the engine's modules. The modules of the output are the modules of the state and the engine's modules, by ID: a module with a state has the start band, both marks of the series and the skills; an engine's module without a state with a current version has the starting point by grade, `BandByGrade`, and when that version has no bands, only its ID, a zero `StartBand` and `HasEvidence: false`; an engine's module without a current version is not in the output: it has no bands, and the engine will not get its graph. Each module must have an entry in the "graphs", and a graph is checked as in `Append`; otherwise, `ErrInvalid`. A reader of `ModuleReadout` takes the start band only when its place is 1 or more. `BandByGrade` still rejects an empty list: a piece of evidence without bands has no start band;
  - the rule "no starting point" of `Read` and `Readout.Task` is one function, `startingPoint`, and `Readout.Task` rejects a candidate of a module without a state and without bands with the name of the task and the module. `checkGraph` rejects a skill taught on a band that the graph does not have;
  - the module's traps are in the output of `Read` at the module, as a skill's traps are at the skill: a trap without skills with a state is counted only there, and which are frequent is decided by `engine`;
  - `Readout` holds a copy of the state, the graphs, t₀, the grade and the modules whose graphs `Read` checked, and `Readout.Task` computes by them without checking these graphs again. An item's P is computed by one function, `chanceOf`, both for the step and for `Task`, and in the trial series it is as at the step, R̄ = 1: the P of reading is the P that the step would take for a piece of evidence at t₀. The step and reading take a new module by one function, `newModule`, with θ = θ₀, so the P of the first piece of evidence at the step is the same as at reading. The provenance of the key and the tags does not change P: a piece of evidence at weight 0 moves nothing, but the probability of the answer is the same.
- **Decided apart:** how "mastered" goes by `retired_skills`, and with it knowing after a major version of the module that teaches it — the evidence and the log; the types of reading. What the engine does at "no evidence", when knowing goes away and at a cycle of prerequisites between modules through anchor skills is `engine`'s. Knowing by skills with `rubric` waits for the answer to О-17.

— *Why:*
- **"Or" over the skills, the threshold of "mastered".** A skill that teaches an anchor skill teaches it whole: "a learner who has the skill can do what the description of each says", as the module format says, and "to know a skill" in `spec`'s vocabulary is exactly "mastered". A second threshold beside the threshold of "mastered" would be a second number in the same library, and the model keeps one.
- **Any band.** The bands of different modules are not comparable, and the model's level is per module, and the format does not say which band a module that rests on the anchor skill expects. In the first slice anchor skills are taught only by school maths of grade 3, and it has one band.
- **"Does not know" only by the engine's modules.** A module that the engine does not get — disconnected or not suitable for the learner — would hold the dependent skills back forever: the engine will give no sessions on it. `api` named the same reason when it moved knowing into reading. And "mastered" in a disconnected module was derived by evidence, and it stays true: removing knowing by disconnecting would mean that the engine may offer again a module mastered to the end.
- **A reason for each state.** A grade 5 learner without school maths gets "unknown", and the lesson is not held back, and after connecting it, "does not know": connecting a module would make the engine stricter. The reason "no evidence" leaves this case to the rule of `engine`, and the grade does not give knowing: "The grade decides one thing: where the child starts", in `spec`'s vocabulary.
- **Without forgetting.** Time does not remove "mastered", nor knowing derived from it: otherwise after the holidays the skills of another module would close until a repetition in the first. The forgetting of a skill that teaches it is seen in its retention and review date.
- **No state for `rubric` and `none`.** They have no evidence: an attempt with a rubric does not give it (О-17), and `none` is only ever supporting, and an answer gives it nothing. "Unknown" does not hold a lesson back, and in skills without a verdict the engine follows the author's order anyway.
- **At reading.** "Does not know" and "unknown" depend on the set of the engine's modules — on the connections and the profile — not on the log, and "knows" on the graphs that came at reading. They cannot be in the cache, as `api` and `spec` decided.
- **The marks of the series in the state.** `engine` needs them for its choice. The piece of evidence that ends the series clears its answers, and by n after it this can no longer be told.
- **A module without bands in the output.** The format lets a module without tasks leave out `bands`, and `NewGraph` accepts such a graph. `engine` rejects an engine's module without the model's reading and does not read the start band, so skipping the module would break the choice, while an empty start band does not. The skills of such a module still teach anchor skills, but without a band where they are taught they cannot be mastered — "unknown"; a skill taught on a band that the graph does not have would otherwise pass for a skill that can be mastered.
- **The mastery band by ID.** The contract of the skill map names the mastery band by ID, and `api` does not look it up itself.

— *Rejected:*
- "and" over the skills that teach it — each teaches the anchor skill whole; an own cautious probability with an own threshold — a second threshold beside "mastered"; "mastered" only on the highest band where the skill is taught — the bands of modules are not comparable, and the format has no field "which band is needed";
- everything only by the engine's modules: after a mastered module is disconnected the anchor skill would become "unknown", and the engine could offer the module again; everything by the graphs that came, without a new input: a disconnected module with an unmastered skill would hold the dependent skills back forever;
- three states without a reason — with one more module the engine would become stricter; "unknown" while the skills that teach it have no answers — the lesson is open at first and closes with the first answers;
- knowing only while the review date of the skill that teaches it has not come: after a break the skills of another module would close until a repetition in the first;
- "does not know" for an anchor skill taught only by skills without a verdict — it would hold the dependent skills back always; a state with a time of practice for `rubric` and `none` — there is no evidence, and a supporting skill gets no practice either;
- knowing in the cache — it depends on what the cache's mark does not hold;
- knowing by answers where the anchor skill is a supporting skill of the task: a supporting skill gets no step — a shift only upward at a correct answer would pull the estimate up without measure;
- rewriting the mark of the series by any piece of evidence of a module with a state: a piece of evidence at weight 0 would change the state for the first time;
- an item's P in the trial series always with retention at t₀: reading and the step would diverge for skills practised long ago;
- `ErrInvalid` for an engine's module whose current version has no bands: one such module would fail reading for the whole learner; skipping it, like a module without a current version: the engine's choice would break; a `NewGraph` that rejects a graph without bands: a module of lessons alone could not be given to the engine.

— *Consequence:* [architecture.md](architecture.md); `CLAUDE.md`; `README.md`; `readout.go`, `step.go`, `state.go`, `fold.go`, `graph.go` and their tests; `proto/novatrail/learnermodel/v1/state.proto`; `testdata/read/`; [run/DEBT.md](../run/DEBT.md).

## Evidence and the log

**R08. A piece of evidence carries everything that the recomputation reads — the observations of the attempt, not the model's conclusions — and its form only grows: an earlier version reads a new form, skipping what it does not know. The log holds one more kind of fact beside an attempt, an adult's correction of a module's level. The state is a fold of the log: an append is the same step as the recomputation and gives the same bits on any build of one version of `learner-model`. There is no snapshot. The evidence of a retired skill goes to each of its replacements with the share in full, and the replacement derives "mastered" from the transferred evidence. The record of a choice also stores the grade, and a major version of a module does not change the places of the earlier bands.** How it is built is in [architecture.md](architecture.md), its sources in [sources.md](sources.md).
- **What is decided:**
  - the appended state matches the recomputed one bit for bit. The state is a fold of the log in the order of `api`'s numbers: the recomputation is the fold from the empty state, an append is one of its steps over the cache. The bits are the same on any architecture, GOAMD64 level, processor and Go version for one version of `learner-model`;
  - there is no snapshot: `api`'s cache is the state at the end of the log, and a repeat of a choice recomputes the log up to the number that the choice stores;
  - the record of a choice also stores the grade by which the model computed the starting point of the modules without evidence;
  - the recomputation reads a band's place as recorded: the scale of bands is one for all versions of a module. A major version does not change the places of the earlier bands, and a new band stands only after the last one;
  - the transfer by `retired_skills`: each replacement gets the retired skill's share in the step in full — for a split skill each part gets its whole history —, the skills merged in one item count as one skill, and only a skill with `verdict` serves as a replacement. A replacement's "mastered", stability and traps are derived from the transferred evidence by the same rules, and the flag "mastered" does not carry over separately. An item left without primary skills after the transfer moves only θ. How the transfer enters the step — J, the shares, δ̄ and R̄ — is a rule of the weights of evidence;
  - the transfer is one lookup: `module-version.retired-kept` of the module format keeps `replaced_by` on the skills of the current version, and `NewGraph` keeps the same, so the graph of the current version names a chain of retirements already flat, and the step searches `Retired` by binary search, without recursion. `Append` therefore rejects a graph where `Retired` is not in the order of IDs or has a repeat, where an entry's `replaced_by` is not in order, where a retired skill is still a skill of the graph or where a replacement is not a skill of the graph; a graph from `NewGraph` is never like that;
  - without a transfer the bits are as without the rule: the share of the step of δ is `w · (S − P) · k / J`, and the δ and R of a recorded skill are the mean over its replacements, in the order of IDs; at k = 1 and one replacement, multiplication and division by 1 are exact. Against a log recorded under the new IDs the transfer matches bit for bit when a split skill has two parts and no more than two skills merge in one item: (δ + δ) / 2 = δ and x · 2 / 2 = x exactly; with three parts or three merged skills, the mean and the share k/J may depart from such a log by the last bit. The append and the recomputation go by one graph, so they match anyway;
  - a trap's skills go by the same mapping and without repeats: a trap two of whose skills merged into one is counted at it once. A trap's skill that the graph does not know or that has become `rubric` goes to nobody, even if its state is left in the cache. A cache with a skill that the graph has now retired happens only if the graph changed under the same mark of the cache, while `api` recomputes with a new release; `Append` does not touch or check such a skill;
  - a piece of evidence carries everything that `spec`'s list of evidence names and everything that the model reads — as observations, not as the conclusions of the weights of evidence. For an item: whether it was submitted, the reasons of its submissions without a verdict, the first verdict and the number of wrong answers before the submission that gave it, the verdict at the end and the number of wrong answers before the first "correct". Also the supporting skills, the band's ID beside its place and the places of the items for each trap. The piece of evidence does not carry the lesson's segment, the mode, AI's candidate traps or the work;
  - an adult's correction of a module's level is evidence of kind 3 with `level_set_by_adult`: a fact, not a conclusion — the band the adult set and the time. Its module is a part of its task ID, as an attempt's is, and it carries no answer, so an earlier version, which skips the field, reads it as evidence with no answer, which moves nothing;
  - the form of a piece of evidence changes only by adding an optional field or a new value, and each version of the library reads a piece of evidence of any form: it skips an unknown field, counts an item with an unknown check kind or verdict as an item without an answer, and an unknown outcome as "unchecked". A field that a piece of evidence of an earlier form does not have is read as "not recorded", not as "no" and not as 0, and how to read it is decided by the version that added the field;
  - the step of an append reads only the graph of the module of its own piece of evidence. The input "graphs" names for the module of each appended piece of evidence — at the recomputation, all the modules of the log — the graph of the current version or "no current version", and a module of a piece of evidence without an entry is an error. A piece of evidence of a module without a current version moves nothing and waits, as `api` decided;
  - the start band is proposed by the library's rule "band by grade" — by the grade from the profile and the bands of the version on which the attempt went —, and an append replaces it with the band from the state, if the module already has one, and gives the piece of evidence with the band it took. At the recomputation, too, the step takes the band from the state for a module with a state, and the band of the piece of evidence for a module without a state;
  - the thresholds n < 5 and m ≥ 5 are compared with a tolerance of 1e-9, and it is right while an attempt has no more than 170 items with an answer;
  - the time of practice is in whole seconds UTC. The model takes the module and the time of practice from the piece of evidence, and the columns of the same names serve `api` itself;
  - the encoding of the evidence and the state is set by the library, canonically, and `api` stores the bytes as they are;
  - the model does not read anchor skills in the evidence, and it does not need the dictionary of `api`'s format window yet.
- **Decided apart:** how the code keeps the same bits — the build for the device; the types and the encoding. How the engine and `api` repeat a choice with an earlier version of `learner-model` is theirs, and the format of the export `spec`'s. The fields of stage 5 — the provenance of the band and the difficulty of a task from paper — wait for О-11.

— *Why:*
- **Bit for bit.** The thresholds — "mastered", n < 5, m ≥ 5, failures in a row — make the state discontinuous: a difference in the last bit sets or does not set "mastered", and from there the states diverge forever. A tolerance on the numbers holds nothing here. A fold gives a match by construction if the step is deterministic and the cache is stored without loss. The step must be deterministic between builds too: while a rollout goes on, the earlier and the new image append under one mark ([api@d896943](https://github.com/altnovatrail/api/blob/d896943/docs/decisions.md)), and the mark does not carry the build. Go itself does not promise the same bits: "An implementation may combine multiple floating-point operations into a single fused operation" (sources 4.1). The compiler fuses a multiplication with an addition on arm64 and on amd64 at GOAMD64 ≥ 3, and `math.Exp` on amd64 chooses its path by the processor right at run time (sources 4.3). But "An explicit floating-point type conversion rounds to the precision of the target type, preventing fusion that would discard that rounding" (sources 4.2): the same bits are reachable by the discipline of the code, and that is the build for the device.
- **Without a snapshot.** The recomputation is needed only with a change of the cache's mark, and a snapshot under the earlier mark is then of no use, and the transfer does not apply to it. A snapshot for a repeat of a choice would be one more place where the state can diverge from the log. And the recomputation is linear in the log: a one-off Node script, a rough model of the step, recomputed 10,000 pieces of evidence in 13–18 ms.
- **The grade in the choice.** The inputs of reading are the cache, the graphs, the time, the grade and the engine's modules. The choice stores the digests of the graphs, by which the engine's modules are restored too, and the time ([api@d896943](https://github.com/altnovatrail/api/blob/d896943/docs/decisions.md)), but not the grade. Without it the first choice in a module without evidence, whose starting point goes by grade, will not repeat if the grade has changed since.
- **Bands as recorded.** The log is not rewritten, and the recomputation does not open packages of earlier versions: a band's place is all it knows about it. While the places of the earlier bands do not change, the shift 2.5 · (p − 1) of the earlier pieces of evidence is the same. The band's ID beside its place is for a table of correspondence, should one ever be introduced: it cannot be appended to the log after the fact.
- **The share in full.** The module format says: "the evidence of a retired skill counts for each skill that replaced it". With the share in full, the δ of each part of a split skill goes exactly as the δ of the former one, an item's δ̄ and R̄ do not change, even beside other primary skills, and a part's "mastered" comes from the same history. The skills merged in one item are one skill, as if they had been recorded so: otherwise the item's count and practice would double. A replacement without `verdict` has no state, as no skill without a verdict has. The flag "mastered" does not carry over separately, because it is a function of the evidence, and the recomputation derives it anew, and with it knowing the anchor skill that the replacement teaches. An item without skills moves θ: the level belongs to the module, and the task's band and difficulty remain, so the trial series does not change with a major version either.
- **Observations, not conclusions.** The log is not rewritten, and what a piece of evidence does not carry, a new version will no longer read from the old pieces of evidence. Observations — submissions, verdicts, the number of wrong answers — are read by the weights of evidence and later, perhaps, by partial credit (Д7), while a conclusion of the weights, "blank" or "not solved", would be read only by the rule that drew it. `spec`'s list of evidence is the agreement by which `api` builds a piece of evidence. Beyond it and beyond what is read, a piece of evidence carries little for future versions: the IDs of bands, the supporting skills and the places of the items for traps — this is the module's content, not the learner's data —, and the number of wrong answers before the first "correct", without which partial credit cannot be introduced later. Nothing more: data are kept to a minimum. The link of a lesson, which `api` asks about, does not change the weights, and it has no reader — so `spec` refused to record what "has no reader until `learner-model` asks for it". The rule of the transfer of anchor skills presupposes the supporting skills in the evidence.
- **Reading a new form.** `api` rolls back only forward — a pull request with the earlier code (Т-19) —, and an earlier version of `learner-model` will recompute a log where pieces of evidence of the new form already are. `spec`'s module index decides the same: a reader "reads these members of an index of any version by their places, not by the schema of its own version". Standalone did not read a profile newer than itself: "an old instance rewriting a new file would quietly drop whatever it did not understand" (sources 1.9). Here nobody rewrites the log, and reading it while skipping what is unknown loses nothing.

— *Rejected:*
- a match with a tolerance — the thresholds make the state discontinuous; bit for bit only on one build, with the build in the cache's mark — each change of the image would recompute all the logs, and a repeat of a choice on another architecture could diverge;
- a snapshot every N pieces of evidence — it is built anew with each change of the mark and can diverge from the log; a snapshot at each choice — as many copies of the state as there are choices, against keeping data to a minimum;
- a repeat without the grade — the first choice in a new module will not repeat after a change of grade; the start band of the modules without evidence in the record of a choice — a number of the model in `api`'s record instead of the profile's data, one per module;
- a band's place without a rule in `spec` — the comparison of versions would learn of the rule only when some module changes its scale; a band by ID, not by place — the format does not promise that a band's ID will not be taken for another one;
- equal parts between the replacements — the parts would start below the skill they came from and would need more answers before "mastered", though the learner learned nothing anew; the share in full but without "mastered" — knowing an anchor skill would go away too until new answers; the contribution of each recorded skill separately — for a merged skill the count and practice of one item would double; "as if recorded under the replacements" literally — each part of a split skill would get half;
- a piece of evidence with only what the model reads — the facts of `spec`'s list without weight would have to be removed from `spec`, and partial credit by old pieces of evidence could not be introduced; everything the attempt stores — the log would repeat the attempt, and there would be more data;
- a ban on rolling out an earlier version after a new form — `api` would stay on a version with a found error; refusing to compute such a log — the learner would have no model until a new rollout;
- a start band that `api` takes from reading at the start of an attempt, without replacing it at the append — the first two attempts of one module on two devices, with a change of grade between them, would carry different bands;
- the time of practice only as a column of `api` — a piece of evidence carries everything that the recomputation reads.

— *Consequence:* [architecture.md](architecture.md); `CLAUDE.md`; `README.md`; `evidence.go`, `fold.go`, `step.go`, `graph.go`, `types.go` and their tests; `transfer_test.go`; `proto/novatrail/learnermodel/v1/`; `testdata/fold/`, `testdata/wire/`; [run/DEBT.md](../run/DEBT.md).

## The build for the device

**R09. The library gives the same bits on any build of one of its versions — by the Go compiler, on any architecture, GOAMD64 level, processor and Go version, in WebAssembly too — and is built for the device as it is. This is held by the discipline of the code: the result of each multiplication and division of floating-point numbers at once passes an explicit `float64()`, and exp and log are its own, a port of pure Go from the package `math`. The library imports only Go's standard library, by a list. CI checks the same bits from the first code — by runs on amd64 at GOAMD64 v1 and v3, without processor extensions, on 386, in js/wasm and wasip1 and on arm64, by a differential check of the assembler where the compiler fuses operations and by the rules of code. By Т-10, compute on the server, and how it works offline is decided by stage 5.** How it is built is in [architecture.md](architecture.md), its sources in [sources.md](sources.md), the experiment in [measurements.md](measurements.md).
- **What is decided:**
  - the promise is for the compiler `gc`, on any architecture it knows, 32-bit too, at any GOAMD64 and GOARM64, on any processor and in any Go version not lower than the one `go.mod` names. TinyGo and gccgo are outside the promise. The assumption is the default floating-point environment: Go does not change it, but C code through cgo could;
  - the dependencies are only Go's standard library, by a list, both in the code and in the tests; `go.mod` without `require` and `replace`, and no nested modules but those the lists name. From `math`, only functions with an exactly defined result. exp and log are a port of pure Go from `math`, with `float64()` at each step, and the BSD-3-Clause notices of Go and Sun Microsystems are kept in the file of the port. The list of the code's packages is in [architecture.md](architecture.md), and a new package enters it by a change of this entry;
  - the rules of code are eight, as [architecture.md](architecture.md) lists them: `float64()` at each `*` and `/`; a list of functions of `math`; own exp, log, σ, power and retention in the public package `detmath`, so that `engine` takes the same port; neither NaN nor ∞; numbers into integers only with a range check, and what is stored and the time are `int64`; a canonical order without iterating over maps and with a byte-by-byte comparison of IDs; neither global mutable state nor goroutines; imports only from the list, without cgo, `.s`, `//go:linkname` and build tags;
  - rule 1 — `float64()` at each `*` and `/`, neither `*=` and `/=`, nor `complex`, nor type parameters in arithmetic, in `++` and `--` too — holds in the tests too: their inputs must be the same on all targets. The functions of `math` for the tests are the list of the code plus `Exp` and `Log`, the reference of accuracy. In the code, but not in the tests, the check also holds no iteration over a map — also over a type parameter whose type set holds a map —, no function that reads tables made from Unicode — for `strings` the tables of the package `unicode` (`ToLower`, `EqualFold`, `Fields`, `TrimSpace` and the like), for `strconv` its own tables of printable characters (`Quote`, `IsPrint` and the like) —, and no statement `go`. A function is recognized by its object, not by how it is written: `Exp` after `import . "math"` is `math.Exp` too. A file name that the command `go` reads as a build constraint is a build tag, as is a line `//+build` without a space; besides `.s`, no other sources that the command `go` would hand to something other than the Go compiler are allowed either — `.sx`, `.c`, `.syso` and the like;
  - CI from the first code, by the shared jobs of `go.yml` of `.github` with the input `same-bits`, of the release that `.github/workflows/ci.yml` pins: "Go / targets" — the tests on amd64 at GOAMD64 v3, at v1 with `GODEBUG=cpu.all=off`, at GOARCH=386, in js/wasm through Node and in wasip1 in `node:wasi` of the same Node; "Go / fma-diff" — the differential check of the assembler; "Go / arm64" — `ci-test` and the runs of the targets for its host on `ubuntu-24.04-arm`; "Go / rules" — the rules of code by `.github`'s tool on `go/types`, with the lists of this repository in `.github/coderules.json`, which also name the nested module `.devcontainer/gopls`. The recipes `ci-test` and `ci-lint` are called by the same wrapper, and buf runs in the repository's own job "Proto" of `ci.yml`. The golden vectors are the state by the log, the output of reading and the table "input → bits" of exp, log, σ, power and retention; they change only with the version of `learner-model`;
  - the differential check: at `-d=fmahash=n` all the fusion rules are off, so an identical assembler means the same as "nothing to fuse", and the build also prints "fmahash triggered" at each place where a rule would have fired. The control with `a*b + c`, `x/4 + y` and `c − a*b` belongs to `go.yml`, and the control that breaks each rule of code of this repository to `.github`'s tool;
  - a power with a fractional exponent has exact branches s = 0 and s = 1: at S̄ = 1 and 0 the rule of τ by the share of correct answers coincides exactly with the rules for all correct and none correct, while exp(1 · log a) need not coincide with a;
  - the clamp of rule 4 in `detmath`: `Exp` and `Sigmoid` take [−708; 708], where exp and σ are finite and normal on both sides: σ(−708) ≈ 3.3e-308, while at −709 and below 1 / (1 + e⁷⁰⁹) is already subnormal; `Log` takes the positive normal numbers; `Retention` takes Δt in [0; the largest `float64`], so that a negative Δt — time that came backwards — gives 1, as the "Δt = max(0; …)" of forgetting does, τ in the positive normal numbers, so that 0 and a negative τ give a retention of almost 0, not 1, and F · Δt / τ no greater than the largest `float64`. The clamp sends NaN to the lower bound — in `Sigmoid` too, though −NaN does not change it: the function returns a number, not an error, and does not drop the step, though NaN never reaches it — the encoding rejects it;
  - `Pow` clamps the base and the exponent before the branches: the base as `Log` does, the exponent to the finite numbers. a¹ = a for any a in the domain of `Log`, and 0¹ is 2⁻¹⁰²², not 0; s · ln a is never 0 · ∞, so 1ˢ = 1 for any s, ±∞ too. The bases of the τ rule — 1 + g · (1 − R) ≥ 1 and 1/2 — are far inside;
  - F is a constant of `detmath`, `RetentionFactor` = 19/81, and C = 1/2 is the square root of the formula: retention is exactly these two constants, and `engine`, if it takes `Retention`, takes them with it. R(τ, τ) is 0.8999999999999999, not 0.9: 1 + 19/81 is not exact in `float64`;
  - a number to an integer in the port is `truncate`, with a range check by rule 5: within ±2³⁰ — as `int(x)`, beyond them — saturation, NaN — 0. The port calls it with |x| < 1100;
  - the port's NaN is the bits of `math.NaN`, `0x7FF8000000000001`, through `Float64frombits`: `math.NaN` is not on the list of rule 2, and with it the port equals `math.Log` bit for bit on a negative argument too;
  - the tests of `detmath` compare the port with `math` bit for bit on 386 and in wasm, and on the other builds to within 2 ulp, except at the edges of overflow, NaN with a payload and subnormal arguments of `Log`, where `math`'s own assembly on amd64 departs from the correct answer and the port does not (measurements 1.2). There are two golden files: a table "input → bits" and the SHA-256 of the bits on 100,000 PCG inputs for each function; `DETMATH_UPDATE=1` writes them, not a flag: the package `flag` is not on the tests' list;
  - the glue with the app — `syscall/js` or `//go:wasmexport` — goes in a separate package and only at stage 5;
  - Т-10 stays open, its recommendation supplemented: compute on the server; `learner-model` keeps the build for the device and the same bits, and CI checks them from the first code; how it works offline — including the variant where the server chooses the queue in advance and the device only writes pieces of evidence — is decided by stage 5; `engine` follows the same discipline.
- **Decided apart:** the types, the operations and the encoding; how it works offline — stage 5, by Т-10; the discipline of the code of `engine` and the repeat of a choice on the device — `engine`'s; a run on targets that are not in CI — ppc64, ppc64le, s390x, riscv64, loong64, arm, mips and builds with software floating point — Д10.

— *Why:*
- **The same bits are reachable.** IEEE 754 addition, subtraction, multiplication, division and square root are correctly rounded, so each operation gives one result on any target. Go adds one liberty to this — "An implementation may combine multiple floating-point operations into a single fused operation, possibly across statements", — and "An explicit floating-point type conversion rounds to the precision of the target type, preventing fusion that would discard that rounding" (sources 4.4). The functions of `math` with their own assembly and the conversions that the specification leaves to the implementation also diverge. The rules of code close all three.
- **The experiment confirmed it** (measurements 1.1). The port with `float64()` gave the same bits of exp, log, σ, power, retention and the likelihood of the trial series on a grid — on 114,561 and 200,009 inputs — in six builds: amd64 at GOAMD64 v1 and v3, v1 with `GODEBUG=cpu.all=off`, 386 and js/wasm. On 386 and in wasm the port equals `math.Exp` and `math.Log` bit for bit. But `math.Exp` on amd64 diverges from the port by up to 2 ulp on 18,069 inputs of 114,561, and with `cpu.all=off` on 15,562: the bits change with the processor even for one binary. The experiment's own harness, where `1 + r.Float64()*1000` was left without `float64()`, gave a different retention on v3 — rule 1 in action, and why it holds in test code too.
- **A differential check, not a list of instructions.** The rule `useFMA` stands at each fusion rule of each architecture, and `-d=fmahash=n` turns them all off (sources 4.5). A comparison of two builds does not depend on the names of instructions and will survive new rules and architectures. The experiment: the assembler of `detmath` matched on arm64, ppc64le, s390x, riscv64, loong64 and amd64 v3, and the control diverged on each (measurements 1.1).
- **The rule is for each `*` and `/`, not only for `a*b + c`.** The compiler turns a division by a constant with an exact reciprocal into a multiplication (sources 4.5), so `x/4 + y` fuses too. Fusion goes across operators and through inlining — also into the code of a consumer that `api` builds with its own flags. This can be checked only in the library's sources, and a check on `go/types` holds the rule.
- **Own exp and log.** `math.Exp` has its own assembly on amd64, arm64, loong64, riscv64 and s390x, and on amd64 it chooses its path by the processor; `math.Log` on amd64 and s390x; `math.Pow` goes through them (sources 4.3). The port takes the code that `math` itself runs on wasm and 386, and so matches it in wasm, and its accuracy is the same. Go's license is BSD-3-Clause, and Sun's notice permits copying "provided that this notice is preserved" (sources 4.5): both are compatible with Apache-2.0, and the license check does not see copied code — so the notices stand in the file, and the record is here.
- **Only the standard library.** The Go module of `spec` embeds the whole `format/` (`//go:embed all:format`), and it would get into each build, WASM too, while the library's types are its own. A third-party module is one more place where the bits or the bytes change with its version. Even `encoding/json` in Go 1.27 is built from v2 (sources 4.5), so the bytes of the stdlib encoders are not an agreement between Go versions.
- **CI from the first code.** Otherwise nobody would check the promise "on any build" of the evidence and the log until stage 5, and a violation would hide in the last bit until the first divergence of what is appended from what is recomputed during a rollout of `api`. A run on arm64 is free: GitHub's arm64 runners for public repositories are "free and unlimited", and `learner-model` is public (sources 5.1).
- **Not closing Т-10.** The question is shared with `engine`. And offline has a path that Т-10 does not name: the server chooses the queue in advance, and the device only writes pieces of evidence — then there is no computation on the device at all. The js/wasm build is useful anyway: it is one more compiler backend without fusion, and in it the port is checked against `math`.
- **The bounds of the clamp.** Within them exp, σ and log are finite and normal, so no function of `detmath` gives NaN, ∞ or a subnormal number, whatever comes in; outside them `math`'s own assembly on amd64 departs from the correct answer (measurements 1.2).

— *Rejected:*
- `math.FMA` everywhere instead of separate operations — the same bits too, but on amd64 at v1 and in wasm it is in software and slow, and the differential check would then not tell an intended fusion from an accidental one;
- fixed point — rewriting the whole model, and own exp and log would be needed anyway; a correctly rounded libm — there is none in the standard library; tables of values;
- `-d=fmahash=n` in the consumer's build — a debugging flag of the compiler, and the library does not set the build of `api` and `sim`;
- the build in the cache's mark — not needed: the same bits are reachable;
- third-party modules with a compatible license; the Go module of `spec`; the protobuf runtime — the codec is its own;
- checks of the device only from stage 5; a set without an arm64 runner; a minimum — the js/wasm build and the list of imports;
- closing Т-10 now; leaving its recommendation as it was.

— *Consequence:* [architecture.md](architecture.md); `CLAUDE.md`; `README.md`; `go.mod`; `detmath/`; `.github/coderules.json`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`; `justfile`; [run/DEBT.md](../run/DEBT.md).

## Types and encoding

**R10. The library's types are its own and simple. The graph is per module: the bands in order and the skills with what the model reads, and also with prerequisites and tier for `engine`, while the bands where a skill is taught the library derives itself from the task descriptions. The task description is one for evidence and for a candidate task. Evidence and state are encoded in protobuf's binary form by a `.proto` schema of its own; the codec is its own, on the standard library, and buf checks the schema and the codec in CI.** How it is built is in [architecture.md](architecture.md), its sources in [sources.md](sources.md).
- **What is decided:**
  - the graph carries what the model reads, and beyond that the prerequisites and the tier of a skill: the engine reads them for certain. What else the rules of `engine` need of the graph, `engine` asks for, while lessons and the pool are its own types;
  - the encoding of evidence and state is protobuf's binary form. The codec is its own, on the standard library, without the protobuf runtime; the `.proto` schema is the description of the form; `buf lint`, `buf breaking` and `buf convert` are in CI;
  - the packages — the module root `github.com/altnovatrail/learner-model` with the types and operations, `detmath`, an internal codec `internal/wire/`, and the schema in `proto/novatrail/learnermodel/v1/`, package `novatrail.learnermodel.v1`;
  - the graph's fields — the module, the bands in order with IDs and grades; for a skill — the full ID, whether it is assessable, `teaches`, the bands where it is taught, prerequisites and tier, `retired_skills`; the input "graphs" — module → graph or "no current version";
  - the bands where a skill is taught are derived by `NewGraph`, the function of the library that builds the graph from the module's skills and the descriptions of its tasks: one rule, not a copy in `api` and another in `sim`. They are the bands of the tasks where the skill is primary for an item: by the item's own skills, or by the task's skills for an item without its own and for a task without items; a skill of a task whose every item names its own skills is trained by no item, and that task's band does not count for it. `NewGraph` rejects a duplicate ID and a duplicate within one list — of replacements, anchor skills, prerequisites, grades —, a band's place out of order, a task's band outside the bands or with the ID of another band in its place, a difficulty outside 1–5, a primary skill and a replacement that the module does not have;
  - the task description `Task` — the ID of the task or template, the band by place and ID, the difficulty, the primary and supporting skills, the provenance of the key and of the tags, the items with their skills and answer slots — is one for evidence and for the engine's candidate, while what happened to an item in the attempt is `Evidence.Items`, by place; the codec joins them into one `ItemEvidence` and rejects places that do not match one to one. One function, `checkTaskContent`, checks the task description of the evidence and of the candidate;
  - the operations — `BandByGrade`, `Append`, `Recompute`, `Read` — `Read(s State, g Graphs, at int64, grade Grade, engineModules []string) (Readout, error)` —, `(Readout) Task(Task) (TaskReadout, error)`, `LevelCorrection`, which gives an adult's correction of a module's level as evidence, and the codec; a grade has a separate value "no grade";
  - the Go API is values, without pointers, and a field that may be absent by its meaning is a value with a flag beside it: the grade (`Grade.Known`), the mastery band (`Mastered`), the hint's rung for the outcome "after a hint", the number of options for a choice, the fact "the solution went to the device" — a template has none —, the first and the last verdict and the item's first "correct". The schema holds the same by the presence of `optional`: a field without a value is not written;
  - the input "graphs" is `Graphs`, a map module → `ModuleGraph{Graph, Current}`: the model only reads it by key and does not iterate it. The enums are types of their own over `int32` with the schema's numbers, and a value this version does not know stays a number;
  - the types of the reading's output: the mastery band is `BandRef`, not a place; for a module — `TrialOver`, `LastInTrial`, the module's traps `Traps` and the level an adult set, `LevelBand` with `HasLevelBand`; for a teacher — `Practised` and the highest band `BandRef`; `Readout` with unexported fields for `Task`;
  - errors are values, without panics, of three kinds, which `errors.Is` tells apart: `ErrInvalid`, `ErrCorrupt` and `ErrNotFinite`; the message names what and where. There is no `fmt` in the code: it is not on the list of the code's imports;
  - the rules of the encoding: `optional` on every scalar field, `double` — eight IEEE bytes in little-endian, the writer writes fields by number, `packed`, and the elements of repeated fields in canonical order, no `map<>` fields, the reader skips an unknown number by its wire type and rejects what is corrupt, a field number is never reused;
  - the schema is three files in `proto/novatrail/learnermodel/v1/`: `common.proto` — `BandRef`, `AnswerSlot`, `TrapCount` and the enums `CheckKind`, `Verdict`, `Provenance`; `evidence.proto` — `Evidence`, `ItemEvidence`, `FoundTrap`, `Outcome`, `UnverdictedReason`; `state.proto` — `State`, `ModuleState`, `TrialAnswer`, `SkillState`. The numbers follow the order in which [architecture.md](architecture.md) lists the fields of the evidence and of the state, `kind` first. `CheckKind` holds only the kinds with a verdict: an attempt of a task with a rubric gives no evidence (О-17), and a new kind is an added value. A template task is the flag `template`, an item with its own skills is the flag `own_skills`: an empty list of its own primary skills could not otherwise be told from their absence;
  - field 8 of `ModuleState`, `optional bool last_evidence_in_trial`, `LastInTrial` in Go: whether the last piece of evidence that moved the module was still in the trial series. It is always written, and the reader requires it, as it does fields 1–4;
  - the fields of an adult's correction and of the guard after three failures: `Evidence.LevelSetByAdult`, field 21 of evidence of kind 3; `ModuleState.FailuresInARow` and `TrialEndedByAdult`, fields 9 and 10 of a module of a state of kind 2. As the leading question, field 20 of evidence of kind 2, each is written from its kind on, required there and refused in an earlier kind. A golden correction, `testdata/wire/correction.binpb` with its JSON, joins the golden bytes;
  - a strict writer. It rejects a kind outside 1…`EvidenceKind` or 1…`StateKind`, a string not in UTF-8 — a proto3 `string` field must be UTF-8 —, a duplicate place, skill, trap or module, and a value without its flag: a rung without `HasRung`, a number of wrong answers without a first verdict and the like. Otherwise the value would silently vanish from the log, which is never rewritten;
  - a strict reader. Besides skipping an unknown field by its wire type — a 32-bit one too, though the schema takes none —, the reader rejects: a field of the wrong wire type, a non-repeated field twice, a field that every kind from 1 on writes, if it is missing, a value without its flag's field, kind 0, a string not in UTF-8, the elements of a repeated field not in canonical order or twice — except the answer slots, the reasons without a verdict and the answers of the trial series, whose order is the order of events —, a `bool` other than 0 and 1, an `int32` out of range, groups, field number 0, NaN and ∞. Protobuf reads a repeated field as "the last one wins", but the writer never writes so, and a repeat means corruption. What the reader accepted, the writer returns as the same bytes, except unknown fields. A varint not in its shortest form and fields not in the order of their numbers the reader accepts: the value does not change by it. A kind newer than its own it reads. The wire type is checked in one place, before the value is read, and one generic helper reads the values;
  - the schema follows the rules of `spec`'s protobuf contracts: the package `novatrail.learnermodel.v1`, names are terms in snake_case, an enum's zero value is `<NAME>_UNSPECIFIED`, `buf lint` — the categories `STANDARD` and `COMMENTS` with `disallow_comment_ignores: true`, and the field names in JSON are as in the schema;
  - buf — `buf.yaml` v2 at the root, the module `proto`, without `deps`, as in `spec`; the recipe `ci-proto` and the job "Proto" in `ci.yml`, with tags in the checkout for `buf breaking`. `buf breaking` checks against nothing before the first tag, and from it against the latest tag `v*`, category `FILE`; the latest tag is by version, with `versionsort.suffix=-`, so that a pre-release `v1.0.0-rc.1` comes before `v1.0.0`, not after. The golden bytes `testdata/wire/*.binpb` have their JSON beside them: `buf convert` turns the bytes into this JSON and the JSON back into the same bytes, and so checks the codec against the schema. `just proto-json` writes the JSON, on one line, as buf gives it.
- **Decided apart:** what else the rules of `engine` need in the graph's types — `engine`'s, which it asks of `learner-model`; the format of the export — `spec`'s; the fields of stage 5 — the provenance of the band and the difficulty of a task from paper (О-11).

— *Why:*
- **Prerequisites and tier at once.** The component's card in `spec` names prerequisites for the engine, and `engine`'s own architecture lists for a skill its tier, its prerequisites and whether it is assessable. Without these fields `api` and `sim` would build the graph twice — before `engine`'s detailing and after it. The rest — names, descriptions, lessons — the model's rules do not need, and the card keeps the types simple, not a copy of the format.
- **The bands where a skill is taught come from the library.** It is a rule of the model: the band of a task where the skill is primary for the task or the item. Were `api` and `sim` to derive it separately, the graph of one module could come in two forms — and `spec` gave the types to one library precisely so that the graph is translated into one form.
- **Protobuf's binary form.** Numbers are eight IEEE bytes as they are, so −0 and the last bit survive the encoding with no rules about text. An unknown field is skipped by its wire type, and the rule that an earlier version reads a new form holds for free. A `.proto` schema with comments in English is an open description of the form, which the export awaits, and buf is already in `spec`'s container: `buf breaking` holds "the form only grows", and `buf convert` gives JSON of the same value. Experience: `buf convert` turned a hand-encoded piece of evidence into JSON and back into the same bytes, and `buf breaking` caught a deleted field (measurements 3).
- **Its own codec.** The protobuf runtime for Go is a third-party module, while the library imports Go's standard library alone, and its deterministic output is promised only within one binary: "It is not guaranteed to remain stable over time" (sources 4.8). The recomputation, however, compares the state's bytes between builds.
- **`optional`.** Without it a scalar field in proto3 has no presence, and "not written" would read as 0, while the evidence tells them apart.
- **Values and flags.** The state is copied by assignment, and an append shares nothing with the state it started from — with pointers it would have to copy deeply.
- **A field for the last piece of evidence in the series.** The reading gives whether the module's last piece of evidence was still in the trial series, so that the engine does not work through the error of the series' last answer, and the state did not otherwise remember it.
- **As `spec`'s contracts.** `spec` has already set the platform's rules for protobuf — the packages `novatrail.<part>.v1`, `STANDARD` and `COMMENTS`, a field's name in JSON as its name in the schema, in snake_case —, and `learner-model`'s schema follows them, starting no second style beside the contracts, while the export's JSON writes the same names. `buf convert` can do so with `use_proto_names=true` (measurements 3).

— *Rejected:*
- only what the model reads in the graph: prerequisites and tier would come in a new version after `engine`'s detailing; the whole server part of the graph — the card keeps the types simple;
- the bands where a skill is taught as a field that `api` and `sim` fill by their own copy of the rule;
- JSON by RFC 8785 — readable without tools, but numbers are text through `strconv`, several times more bytes, and the rule "only grows" would be checked by a test of its own; a binary form of its own — neither tools nor a standard, and the export would need its own text about the bytes;
- the protobuf runtime and generated code — above, "Its own codec";
- proto3 without `optional` — "not written" is indistinguishable from 0; a package of its own `learnermodel.v1` and `STANDARD` alone — a second style of protobuf beside `spec`'s contracts;
- the elements of repeated fields in the order of iterating maps — one state would give different bytes;
- the last piece of evidence in the series without a new field, by keeping the series' answers until the next piece of evidence: those are answers after the end of the series, which `Append` rejects, and the meaning of the field of the series' answers would become double; storing n up to the last piece of evidence — a number for the sake of one bit.

— *Consequence:* [architecture.md](architecture.md); `CLAUDE.md`; `README.md`; `types.go`, `evidence.go`, `state.go`, `readout.go`, `errors.go`, `graph.go`, `codec.go`, `codec_check.go`, `codec_read.go` and their tests; `internal/wire/`; `proto/`, `buf.yaml`; `testdata/wire/`; `justfile`; `.github/workflows/ci.yml`.
