## Code before the gate of `sim`

**R12. The model's first version is the baseline: its code and CI come before the first tag of `sim`, and so does its first signed release, checked by its own tests, by the property "appended equals recomputed" and by standalone's test vectors. The gate of `sim` holds every change of the model after it.**
- **Why.** The harness `sim` imports `learner-model` together with `engine` and takes its version by `go.mod`, while the library's CI takes `sim` by tag and puts in the pull request's code through `go.work`. `spec`'s plan put the model's code after the first tag of `sim` — but without a version of the model the harness has nothing to run, and a circle results. The criterion is written before the first run of a candidate, not before the first line of code: the baseline is what the harness compares the first candidate with. standalone's model, which it starts from, passed its own harness, and its numbers — Д5–Д7 — are what the gate checks first.
- **What this means:** the scaffold, CI, `detmath`, the types, the codec and the model come first, then the first release `v0.1.0`, then the gate, once the first tag of `sim` is out. Intermediate versions are not released, and before v1.0.0 the library's API may change, so the types do not wait for `engine`'s detailing.
- **What is outside the repository:** `spec` agreed and recorded it in its plan — the model's first version, the first version of `engine` and the changes of both through the gate are three steps, the first without waiting for `sim` —, and `sim` puts the harness on this version. The gate is the job "Gate" of `ci.yml`, by a tag of `sim`, a required check of the ruleset `main`. The baseline is the first release, `v0.1.0`: the later patches with the same code and schema the gate checks like any commit.

— *Rejected:*
- only what does not change the model — the scaffold, CI, `detmath`, the types and the codec — before the tag of `sim`, and the model after: the circle remains, and the epic stops halfway;
- the whole epic waits for `sim`: the same, and longer still;
- `sim` keeps a copy of standalone's model as the baseline: the model would live in two repositories, and the harness would measure `learner-model` by someone else's code.

— *Consequence:* `RUN.md`; `CLAUDE.md`; `README.md`; [architecture.md](architecture.md); `.github/workflows/ci.yml`.

## Scaffold and CI

**R13. The library has a Go module `github.com/altnovatrail/learner-model` with `go 1.27.3` and no `require`, and CI checks for its code: the recipes `ci-lint`, `ci-test`, `ci-vuln` and `ci-licenses` in the `justfile` are called by the job "Go" — the shared `go.yml` of `.github` with the input `same-bits` —, the coverage goes to Codecov, and CodeQL reads Go. All the references to shared workflows are to one release of `.github`, v0.1.33, commit f1c7e9e. `ci-lint` is `gofmt`, `go vet` and the check of the gopls version, with no other linters.**
- **What is set up:**
  - `go.mod` — `go 1.27.3`: the promise of the same bits holds in any Go version no lower than the one `go.mod` names, and it was checked on 1.27.3. There is no `require` and will be none: the library imports Go's standard library alone. At the root — `doc.go` of the package `learnermodel`, in English: what this library is;
  - the `justfile` — after the pattern of `module-kit`, without its recipes of the release and of the check of `spec`'s modules:
    - `ci-lint` — `gofmt` over the Go files git knows, except `testdata/`, `go vet ./...` and the check of the gopls version of the devcontainer;
    - `ci-test` — two runs: `go test -race -count=1 ./...`, then `go test -count=1 -covermode=set -coverpkg=./... -coverprofile=coverage/go.out ./...`, one Go profile, which the wrapper's job `coverage` sends to Codecov by OIDC, without a secret (sources 5.3);
    - `ci-vuln` — govulncheck v1.9.0, `ci-licenses` — go-licenses v2.0.1 with the list `ALLOWED_LICENSES`, which the wrapper passes. Both are `go run` at an exact version, without installing, outside `go.mod` and outside Dependabot: they are raised by hand (sources 5.6);
  - `.github/workflows/ci.yml` — the job "Go" by the text `.github` gives its callers, with `contents: read` and `id-token: write`; `codeql.yml` — `languages: go`; `.github/codecov.yml` — the template's text, as in `module-kit`: the changed lines covered no less than 90%; `dependabot.yml` — the ecosystem `gomod` by the template, though it has nothing to raise; `.gitignore` — `coverage/` and `dist/`;
  - the references to `secrets.yml`, `dco.yml`, `documents.yml`, `go.yml`, `codeql.yml`, `scorecard.yml` and `release.yml` — to `.github` v0.1.33, commit f1c7e9e (sources 5.9);
  - README — the Go version badge and the coverage badge. The coverage badge went up only once the upload on `main` carried lines: a badge without data is not put up;
  - the ruleset `main` requires, beside the checks of the same bits and the gate, "Go / lint", "Go / test", "Go / vuln", "Go / licenses", "CodeQL / analyze (go)" and "Proto" with `integration_id` 15368, and `codecov/patch` with `integration_id` 254. The executor puts a check in the ruleset through `gh api`, on the author's instruction, after its first green run; `codecov/patch` — once its status has come on a pull request at least once.

— *Why:* without the module, the recipes and the job "Go" the code has nowhere to pass its checks, while tests and `codecov/patch` come with every change of code. What the code adds to the files of CI is written by the repository's own session, by `spec`'s rule. The references are all to one release, so that the files hold one version of the shared checks, and raising them changes one SHA. Codecov does not need turning on: its app is installed in the organization for all repositories, and it turns the repository on with the first upload (sources 5.7). It costs no money: the repository is public, `spec` chose Codecov for public repositories at no cost, and GitHub's standard runners are free for them (sources 5.1).

— *Rejected:*
- raising only some of the references by hand, as `module-kit` once did: the files would hold different releases, and Dependabot's pull requests would have to be merged or closed one by one;
- staticcheck in `ci-lint`: the check of the rules of code catches the rules of the same bits, SonarCloud the code smells, and one more tool outside Dependabot would have to be raised by hand with every Go version;
- a `go` line below 1.27.3, as `go 1.27.0` in `module-kit`: the promise of the same bits would name versions on which nobody checked it;
- the coverage badge before the first line of code: it would show "unknown";
- requiring `codecov/patch` before its status first came: Codecov puts no status on a pull request without lines of the report, a required check that never comes holds the merge of every pull request, and the ruleset's bypass list is empty.

— *Consequence:* `go.mod`, `doc.go`, `justfile`, `.gitignore`, `.github/`; README.md; [architecture.md](architecture.md); the ruleset `main`.

**R14. gopls stands in the devcontainer image at an exact version, v0.24.0, and is built from its own lock file `.devcontainer/gopls/`, as in `module-kit`. This is a difference of `learner-model`'s container from `spec`'s container until `spec`'s template gives gopls to the kind `go`.**
- **What is set up** — the same as in `module-kit`, word for word, except the repository's name:
  - `.devcontainer/gopls/go.mod` — the module `devcontainer/gopls` with `tool golang.org/x/tools/gopls`, and `go.sum` holds the hash of every module of the build. `go mod init` and `go get -tool golang.org/x/tools/gopls@v0.24.0` gave them; `go.sum` matched `module-kit`'s `go.sum` byte for byte, and `go mod verify` — "all modules verified";
  - in the stage `devcontainer` of the Dockerfile — `ARG GOPLS_VERSION=0.24.0`, a `COPY` of the lock file and `go install -mod=readonly tool` into `/usr/local/bin`; `gopls version` must name `GOPLS_VERSION` as a whole line. The stage `toolchain`, in which the checks run, does not change;
  - `devcontainer.json` — the Go extension does not update its tools, does not look for their updates and does not lint on save, and the comment to `context` says what the image takes from its folder `gopls/`. A missing tool — the debugger dlv, say — it still offers to install, without an exact version, and such an offer is declined;
  - `ci-lint` checks `GOPLS_VERSION` against the version in the lock file: CI does not build the stage `devcontainer`;
  - `dependabot.yml` does not take the folder `.devcontainer/gopls/`: gopls is raised by `go get -tool` in it together with `GOPLS_VERSION`.

— *Why:* without gopls in the image the Go extension installs its latest version by itself, not an exact one, against the rule of exact versions. `spec` had already decided to give the kind `go` the same step, but no tag up to v0.9.4 carried it. gopls v0.24.0 is the latest, of 2026-10-14; the license is BSD-3-Clause (sources 5.6), and it is a development tool: it is shipped nowhere and puts none of its own code into what ships.

— *Rejected:*
- waiting for `spec`'s template: until the tag that gives the step the Go extension would install gopls at an unpinned version;
- only forbidding the extension to install tools: no language server until the same tag.

— *Consequence:* `.devcontainer/`, `justfile`, `.github/dependabot.yml`; CLAUDE.md.

— *Superseded:* since `spec` v0.9.16 the template of `spec` gives the step of gopls and its lock file to the kind `go`, and `.devcontainer/` is the recipe's output with no step of its own; the repository keeps the check of the versions in `ci-lint`.

## The first release

**R16. The release of `learner-model` is made by the shared `release.yml` of `.github` on the line `0.1`, starting with `v0.1.0`, when a commit of `main` with green CI changes the code, the schema or what the file is built with. Go consumers take the code as a Go module by tag, and the release's only file is the schema archive `learner-model-proto-<version>.tar.gz`, signed keylessly; `spec`'s `release-verify` checks it. Immutable releases and a ruleset of the tags `v*` are on.**
- **What is decided:**
  - **the release file** is one: the schema archive `learner-model-proto-<version>.tar.gz`. In it is a folder of the same name with `LICENSE`, `buf.yaml` and all the `.proto` from `proto/`, in a tar that every build writes the same way: names in order, without times and owners, mode 0644, gzip without a name and a time. This is the description of the form of evidence and state for those who read them without Go — for the export of a learner's data, which [architecture.md](architecture.md) describes;
  - **the code** — as the Go module `github.com/altnovatrail/learner-model` by the release's tag, through the proxy and the checksum database, as the module of `spec`: `go get github.com/altnovatrail/learner-model@v0.1.0`. It is not a release file, and `release-verify` does not touch it, while `go.sum` and sum.golang.org hold it;
  - **the settings:** immutable releases are on, and the ruleset `v-tags`, id 26770474 — `refs/tags/v*`, `update`, `deletion`, `non_fast_forward`, without bypass, a copy of `module-kit`'s. The executor turned them on through `gh api` before the first release and checked them against the API's answers;
  - **`release.yml`** — as in `module-kit`: `workflow_run` of CI on `main` and a button, permissions `contents: write`, `id-token: write`, `actions: read`, the shared workflow of `.github` v0.1.33 by SHA (sources 5.9). It builds the files in the stage `toolchain-core` of `.devcontainer/Dockerfile` — Node, just, Go and buf — and signs them at once (sources 5.8); `release-files` needs no more than the stage. `RELEASE_LINE` is `0.1`: the line's first release is `v0.1.0`, and before v1.0.0 the API may change;
  - **`paths`** — the code without tests and `testdata/`, `go.mod`, `proto/`, `buf.yaml`, `LICENSE`, `justfile`, `.devcontainer/Dockerfile` whole and `release.yml` itself. Tests, golden files and documents give no release: the module does not change by them for whoever imports it. An edit of what the file is built with — the `justfile`, `release.yml`, the Dockerfile, a move to a tag of `spec` that changes only the stage `devcontainer` — releases a patch with the same code and schema, and a task does not bypass it; consumers take such a patch only when they raise the version themselves;
  - **the recipes** — `release-files <version>`, which the shared release calls, and `ci-release` in the job "Proto": two builds — the same bytes, the archive's content — `LICENSE`, `buf.yaml` and all the `.proto` git knows, as in the tree, and `buf build` from the unpacked archive. `ci-release` does not run beside a ready `dist/`: `release-files` would replace it;
  - **`buf breaking`** from the first tag checks the schema against the latest tag `v*`;
  - **the description:** [README.md](README.md) of `docs/`, section "Releases", holds the line, the file's content, the export, `release-files` and the commands that take and verify a release. The repository's README.md has no section on releases and no command.

— *Why:*
- **A Go module, not a file.** `sim`, `engine` and `api` are code in Go, and `go.mod` with `go.sum` pin the version themselves, while the checksum database keeps the hash of the zip forever. The module of `spec` is already taken so, and a second route would only split the version in two.
- **The file is the schema.** The shared release needs at least one file in `dist/`, and the schema is the only thing needed without Go: for the export, so that the log can be read without the library. The signature and the attestation of an immutable release link it with the tag's commit.
- **A reproducible tar.** The bytes do not depend on the build's time and owner, so the file can be built again from the tag and compared, and `ci-release` holds this from every pull request.
- **The settings before the first release.** `spec`'s check of a release asks it: a repository turns them on before its first release.
- **The Dockerfile whole in `paths`.** The `paths` filter sees files, not stages: without the Dockerfile a raise of Go in the stage with which the release file is built would give no release, though it may change the archive's bytes, and a stage of its own in a file of its own is a difference from `spec`'s recipe that every move would bring in anew.
- **No command in README.md.** A README holds only what the component is and its place in the architecture: agents run the commands, and `docs/README.md` holds them.

— *Rejected:*
- an archive of the module's whole source — it repeats the Go module, which consumers take by tag anyway; both archives;
- turning on the settings by the author's hands — the executor turns them on and checks them against the API's answer, as it does the ruleset `main`;
- a trial run of the shared release with `publish: false` on a pull request — `ci-release` holds the build of the file, and the shared release checks the signature itself after publishing;
- tests and `testdata/` in `paths` — the module zip carries them too, but a release on every edit of a test or a golden file would give consumers versions in which nothing changed for import. An edit of a test comes out with the next release of code, as in `module-kit`, whose `paths` are also without `*_test.go`;
- removing the Dockerfile from `paths`; moving the stage `toolchain` into a file of its own — above, "The Dockerfile whole in `paths`".

— *Consequence:* `.github/workflows/release.yml`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `justfile`; README.md; [README.md](README.md); [architecture.md](architecture.md); the ruleset `v-tags`.

"Go / targets" and "Go / arm64" run the tests in wasip1 too, in `node:wasi`, and "Go / arm64" takes the image and the Go caches by the composite action `toolchain` (sources 5.5, sources 5.9).
- **The lists** (`.github/coderules.json`): the code's imports — the list of [architecture.md](architecture.md), without `math/bits`, which no file imports; the tests' imports — `bytes`, `crypto/sha256`, `fmt`, `math/rand/v2`, `os`, `path/filepath`, `runtime` and `testing`, without `go/token`, `go/parser`, `go/ast`, `go/types` and `go/importer`, which only a rules test of the repository's own needed. `runtime` is needed by the tests of `detmath`: in wasm and on 386 the port is compared with `math` bit for bit; the tests' functions of `math` are `Exp` and `Log`; `requires` is empty; the nested module is `.devcontainer/gopls`.
- **`ci-test`** is two runs, `go test -race` and then the coverage with `-covermode=set` into `coverage/go.out`. "Go / arm64" calls it on `ubuntu-24.04-arm` and then its host's runs, so a job of the repository's own on arm64 would repeat it.
- **The ruleset `main`** requires the four checks of `same-bits` with `integration_id` 15368, beside the other checks of CI.

The lists are the repository's data, while the rules are one. The shared workflows are on one release, so that the files hold one version of the shared checks.

- the field of the leading question and the value `time` in the schema with the move: a change of the model after the baseline goes through the gate of `sim`, there was no gate before the first tag of `sim`, and packages of format 0.3 have neither;
- rebuilding the container: the `ARG`, `COPY` and `RUN` of the step are the same, and so is the image — Rebuild Container does not change it.

— *Consequence:* `.devcontainer/Dockerfile` and the `justfile` are in the release's `paths`, so an edit of them gives a patch of the line `0.1` with the same code and schema. — `.devcontainer/`, `justfile`, `.github/dependabot.yml`; `proto/novatrail/learnermodel/v1/common.proto`, `types.go`, `step.go`, the codec's files and `testdata/wire/`; CLAUDE.md, README.md; [architecture.md](architecture.md).

## The gate of `sim`

**R20. The gate of `sim` is the check "Gate" of `ci.yml`, a required check of the ruleset of `main`: four jobs "Gate / part k", each `sim`'s `just gate-part k 4`, and after them the job "Gate", `just gate-check bands`, all in the stage `toolchain` of `sim`'s devcontainer of the same tag, with the pull request's code in `GATE_LEARNER_MODEL`. `sim` comes from git by the tag and the SHA of its commit, `SIM_TAG` and `SIM_COMMIT` in the `env` of the workflow, and each part checks the tag by `git ls-remote` first. The gate runs on every pull request and every commit of `main`, on `ubuntu-24.04-arm`, with `sim`'s cache of Go and 15 minutes a job, on the latest tag of `sim`, now `v0.1.28`.**
- **What stands** (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`, the jobs `gate-part` and `gate`):
  - `env` — `SIM_TAG` and `SIM_COMMIT`, named once for all five jobs. A part checks out this repository in `lm/` and `altnovatrail/sim` by `SIM_COMMIT` in `sim/`, both without credentials;
  - the step "sim tag" in each part — the tag from `git ls-remote` must name `SIM_COMMIT`, an annotated one through `^{}`, a lightweight one as it is, or the job fails and names both, so a wrong pair stops in seconds. This is how `sim`'s recipe checks the tags of the libraries against `gate.json`;
  - the image of `sim`'s stage `toolchain` and the caches of Go — `GOCACHE` and `GOMODCACHE` — by the steps of `sim`'s `gate.yml` at `SIM_COMMIT` word for word, keyed by `sim/go.sum` and `sim/.devcontainer/Dockerfile`, with `restore-keys`; part 0 alone saves them, on a push to `main`;
  - the parts — `just gate-part k 4`, each its share of the learners into a folder of parts that goes on as an artifact. The recipe writes `go.work` in a temporary folder — `use` of `sim`, of the pull request's code and of `engine` at the commit from `gate.json` —, does not commit it and does not touch the harness's `go.mod`;
  - the check "Gate" — it runs when a part failed too, and then fails; otherwise `just gate-check bands` merges the four parts, refuses a part missing, repeated or of another run, holds the build to `gate.json` and the numbers to their bands, and puts the table of the numbers against the bands into `$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY`, with `pipefail`. Code 0 means the gate is green, 1 that it is red or the input is rejected, 2 an unknown call;
  - the ruleset of `main` requires "Gate" with `integration_id` 15368; the parts are no required check.
- **The tag of `sim`.** The gate stands on the latest tag of `sim`, so that a candidate's red gate means a shift of a measure, not new bands. While a round of the model's candidates is explored and judged, it stays on the tag the round runs on, and a candidate is merged on the tag with the bands that `sim` recorded on its commit, by the order of releases below. It is `v0.1.28`, commit `4daee0159bb223852a1763f6ce3ff6cbf43911bd`: the base of `gate.json` is `learner-model` at df338e5, the head of the pull request with the card's three rules, without a version, and `engine` v0.1.6, the packages of `modules` v0.2.5, and 69 numbers at 250 learners a cell, with the bands recorded on that base; a task of `sim` moves the base to the release of the rules. A new tag is taken once it is checked: by `git ls-remote`, by `gh release view` — immutable and the latest —, by `gh release verify`, by `git diff` of the two tags for what changes for the caller — the recipes of the gate, `internal/gate/`, the input `GATE_LEARNER_MODEL`, the section of `sim`'s README on taking a release and the stage `toolchain` —, and by `just gate exact` from a clone of `sim` at the commit, in the container on the code of `main`, green with every number to the bit. Its record goes into [sources.md](sources.md).
- **The budget.** 15 minutes a job, as in `sim`'s `gate.yml`, is a guard against a hung run; the times are measurements 6 and measurements 8. A gate that does not fit is cut by an edit of `sim`, not by the caller's time.
- **The order of releases.** A candidate that moves the numbers first gets its bands recorded in `sim` at the commit of its pull request and a new tag of `sim`; then `SIM_TAG` and `SIM_COMMIT` are raised here, and the candidate is merged; `sim` then moves its base to the model's release. Neither Dependabot nor a shared workflow raises them: this repository's session does.

— *Why:*
- **The tag and the SHA in `env`.** So `spec`'s rule of the gate and `sim`'s README ask of a caller ([sim@09e5a84](https://github.com/altnovatrail/sim/blob/09e5a84/README.md)): the SHA holds the code, the tag the reader and the check, and a tag moved by mistake does not pass silently.
- **`sim`'s recipes, not a `go.work` of its own.** They already write `go.work`, take `engine` from `gate.json` and check the build and the bands, and `GATE_LEARNER_MODEL` is their input for the caller ([sim@09e5a84](https://github.com/altnovatrail/sim/blob/09e5a84/README.md)). Recipes of its own would be a copy that drifts from them.
- **`sim`'s stage `toolchain`, not one of its own.** The gate checks the line `go` and the Go it is built with, and `spec`'s rule of the gate names `sim`'s devcontainer of the same tag: then Go, just and git are the same as the harness's.
- **Four parts on arm64, with a cache.** The gate was the longest path of CI. The parts give the numbers of `just gate` to the bit, since a learner comes from its seeds alone, and `sim`'s merge holds them together ([sim@0a31313](https://github.com/altnovatrail/sim/blob/0a31313/justfile)). A part splits its share by the runner's processors, so all five jobs run on one kind of runner: arm64, standard and free for a public repository (sources 5.1), where `sim`'s gate runs about a minute faster than on amd64, and whose numbers `sim`'s CI holds to the same bits as amd64's. The cache of Go spares five jobs the build of the bench, and its steps are `sim`'s own, not a key of this repository's.
- **On every pull request.** So `spec`'s rule of the gate asks: an edit of a document will not move the gate, and keeping a list of the paths that call it would mean guessing what the model reads.

— *Rejected:*
- a reusable workflow of the gate, `sim`'s `gate.yml` by its tag or one of `.github`: its check is "Gate / <name>", not "Gate", so the ruleset of `main` would change, or a job "Gate" after it would add a job to the path; and `spec`'s rule of the gate makes the job the caller's own;
- `go mod edit -replace` instead of `go.work`: it edits the harness's `go.mod`, which `spec`'s rule of the gate rejects;
- the gate on amd64 or on both: amd64 is slower by about a minute, and both would repeat what `sim`'s CI holds;
- the gate only on commits that change the code: paths outside the release's `paths` pass the gate anyway, and `spec`'s rule asks for every pull request;
- a tag older than the latest: a candidate's red gate could mean new bands, not a shift of a measure;
- raising the budget in advance: without a measurement that is a number without data.

— *Consequence:* `.github/workflows/ci.yml`; the ruleset of `main`; README.md, CLAUDE.md; [architecture.md](architecture.md); the records of `sim`'s tags in [sources.md](sources.md) and the times of "Gate" in [measurements.md](measurements.md). `ci.yml` is not in the release's `paths`, so an edit of it gives no release.

## The leading question of format 0.4

**R21. The leading question of a task of format 0.4 is help, as a technique on request: the answer after it goes into the step, the counters and the stability as it is, but does not set "mastered". The evidence of kind 2 carries it by a field of its own, `leading_question_on_request = 20`, in Go `Evidence.LeadingQuestionOnRequest`, apart from `technique_on_request`, and this is a fact of the attempt.**
- **What is decided:** `api` gives the question for a task, not for an item, before the first wrong answer. If the field is set, every answer of the attempt is with help, by the rule `helped`, the same as for a technique on request. There is no new number.
- **When:** the field came by a task of its own after the move to the tag of `spec` with format 0.4, through the gate of `sim`, in one pull request and one release, together with the value `time` of `CheckKind`. It came out in a release of `learner-model` before `api` started writing it, and `api` writes the question only with it, not with `technique_on_request`; `spec` recorded the weight, the field and the order.

— *Why:* the question hints at the method more strongly than a technique — that is why the format closed it —, so it weighs no less than a technique, and help is the strongest weight the rules of evidence give without a new number. A field of its own keeps the fact apart: the log is append-only, and if the harness shows that the question must weigh otherwise than a technique, a new version will read it by recomputation.

— *Rejected:*
- the same rule by the field `technique_on_request` — the log would mix the two facts forever, and splitting them by recomputation would no longer be possible;
- nothing, as a technique at the step — the answer after a question that hinted at the method would set "mastered";
- a smaller weight of the answer — one more constant without data, which the weights of evidence avoid.

— *Consequence:* `proto/novatrail/learnermodel/v1/evidence.proto`, `evidence.go`, `step.go`, the codec's files and `testdata/wire/`; README.md, CLAUDE.md; [architecture.md](architecture.md).

## The plan of E3

**R22. Epic E3 is the first candidates of the model through the gate of `sim`. Up to three candidates on the numbers Д5–Д7 go into `sim`'s first judgement, in one round, each by its own draft pull request, and an exploration chooses them — rough runs of the harness in the container. The numbers that `engine` shares — the threshold "mastered" and the boundary of failure ½ — do not go into the first judgement. The test vectors of standalone that pin an old number are removed by the candidate, with a record.**
- **What was found in planning** (`sim` on `main` a1bf796, read 2026-11-09):
  - `sim`'s judgement of a candidate of the model waits for a new pin and tag of `sim` — `modules` v0.1.4 and new paths —, for the deciding run on the candidate's commit through `go.work` and for the candidate's pull request here. `sim` judges no candidates on `v0.1.0`, and the gates of the libraries move to the tag of the new pin. `sim` judges the candidates of one library in a round: a deciding run on the last commit of each one's pull request, one judgement with all of them, and a confirmation only of the chosen one;
  - the judgement is a deciding run, 4,000 learners per cell, then `sim judge` by the model's criterion: the constraints "no worse" and the estimate of the groups. A candidate names its group and its target, the number of changed constants and a new field, if there is one. Of those that passed the simplest is chosen, and the chosen one is confirmed on held-out learners — otherwise the exit is to the base. By `sim`'s measurement on `olympiad-math` 0.1.1 the deciding run of the base takes 6 h 43 min on 20 cores, and that of the engine's ceiling 1 h 31 min;
  - the numbers Д5–Д7 are named constants of `model.go`, but for ρ, the boundary ½, the halving of τ and the guessing of `predicate`, which stand in formulas, and C, hard-wired in `detmath` and in `reviewDate`. There is no constant of the model's version in the code: the cache's mark is the release's tag.
- **What is decided:**
  1. up to three candidates go into the first judgement, each by its own draft pull request: one constant or a coherent set, with its own group of the estimate. All of them go into one round of judgement;
  2. the candidates are chosen by an exploration — rough runs of `sim` on the numbers Д5–Д7, one at a time, on fewer learners than a deciding run. They decide nothing but show which number moves which measure; the result is an entry of the log with the candidates and the rejected ones. The constants step both ways, and the edges — guessing 0 and full weight — only inwards. The rule of τ by the share of correct answers and C do not go into the exploration: the first is a rule, not a number, and C is hard-wired into a formula and into an exported constant of `detmath`, and changing it is the model's design and the API of `detmath`;
  3. the threshold "mastered" goes only in a pair with the target of `engine`'s corridor, and the boundary ½ is read by `engine` too: neither is in the first judgement, Д16 and Д7;
  4. the vectors of standalone that pin an old number are removed by the candidate, with a record in the log. The baseline with them is held by the tag `v0.1.0`, and the new one by the gate and the golden vectors.
- **The order:** the gate on the tag of the new pin of `sim`, the exploration, the candidates, the judgement and the release, the result.

— *Why:* of those that passed, `sim`'s criterion chooses the simplest, so several candidates in one judgement give more chances than one, and a deciding run takes hours. An exploration is far cheaper than a judgement, and without it the judgement would more likely reject all. A pair with `engine` needs its candidate and a paired criterion, and `spec`'s order of releases does not name how to release the boundary ½ together with `engine`; the first judgement does not wait for it. The vectors of standalone are printed for standalone's numbers: a candidate that changes a number is not supposed to pass them, and keeping them on parameters would mean testing code that does not go into the release.

— *Rejected:*
- one candidate — if it does not pass, one more round of judgement is needed;
- candidates by reasoning, without runs — faster, but blind;
- the pair of the threshold and the target of the corridor in the first judgement — it needs synchronous work with `engine`'s session and its candidate;
- the model's constants as an internal set, so that the vectors of standalone run on its numbers — more code, and a separate task would have to go first.

— *Consequence:* RUN.md and `run/` — the epic's tasks, «Когда вернуться» of Д5–Д7, and Д16; `model.go` and the golden vectors of `testdata/`; CLAUDE.md, README.md and [architecture.md](architecture.md).

## The `spec` tag v0.12.0

**R23. `learner-model` stands on `spec` v0.12.0, the latest release. The tag answers all four rows «в реестр spec» of this repository — on the leading question, the check of gopls in `ci-lint`, the gate and the judgement of several candidates —, all by their recommendations, and the rows are closed. It also brings the released format 0.4, but the move does not change the model's code: the field of the leading question and the value `time` of `CheckKind` go by a task of their own, through the gate of `sim`. `.devcontainer/` is the output of the recipe `new-repo`, and it differs from the former one only in the comment of the gopls step.**
- **What the tag brings `learner-model`** (`git -C spec diff v0.9.16 v0.12.0`, read 2026-11-09; `COORDINATION.md`):
  - the answer on the leading question: `spec`'s "as it carries a technique" speaks of the fact being recorded, not of the field. The evidence carries the leading question by a field of its own in `learner-model`'s schema, apart from the technique on request, and `api` writes it only with this field and only after the release of `learner-model` that brings the field in. `spec`'s vocabulary and the component's card say the same — on the weights of evidence, together with `time` without guessing, and on the evidence itself. The contract `novatrail.api.v1` got the fields `GetHintResponse.leading_question` and `OpenAttempt.requested_leading_question`, but that is a conversation between `app` and `api`, not evidence;
  - the answer on the check of gopls: the comment of the gopls step in `repo-template/kinds/go/Dockerfile` now says that after its creation a copy is held only by the repository's own check, if it has one. Here that is the check in `ci-lint`;
  - the answer on the gate: `COORDINATION.md` records "Gate" as standing at `learner-model` and `engine`, and the first candidates as a new row: a new pin of `sim` — `modules` `v0.1.4`, `engine` `v0.1.3` and `learner-model` `v0.1.3` —, a tag of `sim` with it, then the gate here on that tag;
  - the answer on the judgement: `sim` judges the candidates of one library in a round — a deciding run on the last commit of each one's pull request, one judgement with all of them, of those that passed the simplest, a confirmation only of the chosen one. This is the order the first candidates are planned for;
  - format 0.4 came out with v0.10.0, the first release of the line 0.10. The model is concerned by two of its parts, by the format's text `spec/format/README.md`: a task's `leading_question` and the kind `time` — the key `Ч:ММ`, hours from 0 to 23, and the answer is compared by hours and minutes as numbers. `spec` names as `learner-model`'s the weight and the field of the leading question;
  - the rest between the tags — the legal questions of the analysis, the learner's artifacts, development agents, self-hosting, the grounds of processing and consent, DPIA, eval keys and AI providers, the part `deployment/` of `spec` and the workbook page in the format, the edits of privacy, product, repositories and of `spec`'s release recipes — does not concern `learner-model`: the library stores nothing and sees no learner's data but what `api` passes to it. The template `repo-template/files/`, RULES.md, `spec`'s `.devcontainer/`, the documents check and the recipe `new-repo` did not change.
- **What is changed here:**
  - `spec/` — at v0.12.0, commit 5b4640a; `gh release verify v0.12.0 -R altnovatrail/spec` — "Release v0.12.0 verified!", the release is immutable and the latest (`gh release view` and `gh release list`, 2026-11-09). The tag is in CLAUDE.md, README.md and at the top of RUN.md;
  - `.devcontainer/` — the output of `just new-repo-draft learner-model go 3.8 v0.12.0` in a clone of `spec` in a temporary folder, byte for byte (`diff -r`, 2026-11-09). It differs from the former one only in the comment of the gopls step in `Dockerfile`; the `ARG`, `COPY` and `RUN` are the same, and so is the image;
  - [architecture.md](architecture.md) — format 0.4 is out, `time` by the format's text, `spec`'s answer on the leading question, the code by a task of its own, the comment of the gopls step;
  - README.md — format 0.4 is out, and its field and `time` come through the gate; CLAUDE.md — the first candidates in `COORDINATION.md`;
  - RUN.md and `run/` — the four rows are closed, and a row «в реестр spec» says that `learner-model`'s part of the leading question is not taken yet; the debt of format 0.4 says when to return; the epic's tasks of the judgement follow the answer on it.

— *Why now:* `spec` asked `learner-model` to close its rows, and without the move `spec` would see them open at the source.

— *Why without code:* the field of the evidence and the value of `CheckKind` are a change of the model after the baseline, and it goes through the gate of `sim` by its own pull request and its own release. A task of the move that changes the schema would release a new model in one release with the container's files and the documents, and the gate still waited for a new pin of `sim`.

— *Rejected:*
- v0.10.0, v0.10.1 or v0.11.0: format 0.4 is already in v0.10.0, but the answers to the four rows are not there until v0.12.0, and a move takes the latest release;
- the field of the leading question and `time` in this task — above is why without code;
- rebuilding the container: the `ARG`, `COPY` and `RUN` are the same, and a comment does not change the image.

— *Consequence:* `.devcontainer/Dockerfile` is in the release's `paths`, so an edit of it gives a patch of the line `0.1` with the same code and schema. — `spec/`, `.devcontainer/Dockerfile`; CLAUDE.md, README.md, RUN.md, `run/QUESTIONS.md`, `run/DEBT.md`; [architecture.md](architecture.md).

## The exploration of E3

**R24. Three candidates went into `sim`'s first judgement, all in the group `forgets`, without a target: g = 10 — one constant; the base of the division of τ 0.35 instead of 0.5 — one; and their coherent set — two. An exploration chose them — rough runs of the harness on 15 variants of the numbers Д5–Д7, one at a time, at 100 learners per cell, and then of the four best and the coherent set at 300. The judgement ended with an exit: all three are better than the base by the estimate, but none holds all the constraints "no worse", so the base stays — g = 15 and the halving of τ —, and the numbers stay in Д6. The other numbers are rejected with what the exploration showed, and the harness does not measure the guessing of `predicate`.**
- **The harness** — `sim` `v0.1.2`, commit e61f90a, the tag the gate stood on: `modules` v0.1.4 with `olympiad-math` 0.1.1, `engine` `v0.1.0` from `sim`'s `go.mod`, the set `working`, the rule `engine`, all populations. `sim` had no recipe yet for a run of another `learner-model`, so the run is put together as the gate's recipe puts it: `go.work` in the session's folder with a clone of `sim` and a copy of `learner-model` at the variant's commit, 20 parts `sim run -learners N -part k/20 -commit github.com/altnovatrail/learner-model=<sha>` and `sim merge`. The base is the code of `main` 4f3cfbb. The variants are a commit each on a branch of the clone in the session's folder; neither they nor the files of the runs go into the repository. The SHA-256 of the base: at 100 learners `700bf41b03673fad…20e`, at 300 `6dc6a481c32d8182…be8`; a repeat of the variant "base 0.35" at 100 learners gave the same file, `b70827557e181bff…b69`, to the byte.
- **The analysis** — `sim judge` of the rough look, "a rough look, which decides nothing": a variant stands in the file of the judgement as a candidate for each of the eight groups of the model's criterion and for each target, and `sim compare` gives the differences by measure. The overall score is read only when the candidate's run reads every number with a path (`judge.go`, `score`), and at 100 learners it is not read for any variant, nor for the base against itself: stage 1 ranks by the score of the groups and by the constraints, and the overall score is at 300.
- **Stage 1**, 100 learners per cell — the score of the groups (↑ and ↓ — the interval is wholly above or below zero) and the number of violated constraints "no worse" and of the floor:

A variant's run took from 10 to 45 minutes on 20 cores — longer when the host was busy from outside.
- **Stage 2**, 300 learners per cell — five runs, up to the five best: the four best variants of stage 1 and the coherent set of the two numbers that move `forgets` in the same direction. The best are those that have at least one group improved beyond the noise and no more than ten constraints violated. Not selected: ρ 1/2 — `forgets` +0.372↑, but `learns` and `off-band` ↓ and 20 violations; I 0.202148 — 9 violations, but no group moved beyond the noise, and `forgets` −0.117:

  | g 10 | +0.052 [0.018; 0.093] | +0.396 [0.148; 0.702] | within the noise | 4: `E1` on G2, `M1` on G3-drop, `M5.caught` and `M10` on F-none of the school one — all barely past the tolerance |
  | base 0.35 | +0.039 [0.006; 0.079] | +0.315 [0.060; 0.636] | within the noise | 1: `M10.retention` on F-none of the school one, 0.00245 at the tolerance 0.00202 |
  | σ₀ 3.0 | +0.006 [−0.026; 0.042] | +0.089 | `learns` +0.029↑, `off-band` +0.062↑, `still` −0.015↓, `pages` −0.020↓ | 28 |
  | ρ 1/4 | −0.038 [−0.098; 0.020] | −0.283↓ | `learns` +0.026↑, `jumps` +0.020↑, `off-band` +0.020↑ | 18 and the floor `M4` on G0 of the school one: 0.26 at the floor 0.20 |

  No target — `lag`, `corridor`, `settle` — is reached by any variant; the base is far from them: on G3 the share of those "not settled" is 0.88 for the olympiad one and 0.59 for the school one, at the target 0.25.
- **What is decided:**
  1. three candidates go into the judgement — `g10`: `stabilityGrowth` 10 instead of 15; `halve035`: the base of the division of τ 0.35 instead of 0.5; `forget-set`: both. The group of all of them is `forgets`, there is no target, no new field; constants — 1, 1 and 2. Of those that passed `sim` chooses the simplest, so the set wins only if its score is above the single ones' beyond the noise;
  2. rejected: `bandStep` — both ways it breaks the error of the estimate `M1` and the corridor `E1` on olympiad maths — dozens of constraints —, and 3.0 besides worsens `forgets`; σ₀ — 2.0 worsens `off-band`, and 3.0 at 300 learners is no better than the base and violates 28 constraints; I — in neither direction moves groups beyond the noise; τ₀ — worse both ways, and 2 days worsens `forgets` too; g 22.5 and base 0.7 — within the noise; ρ — 1/4 worsens `forgets` and breaks the floor, and 1/2 improves `forgets` at the cost of `learns` and `off-band`; the weight 1/2 of an answer with help — worse `learns` and `pages`. The variant took the weight of a repeat of a task too: `helped` counts as help also the solution of this task that has already gone to the device ([architecture.md](architecture.md)), so there is no separate variant of a repeat. σ₀ was rejected by the overall score, before `sim` measured the fairness by the grade, which the base does not hold; measured by it, σ₀ comes back only together with the step after the trial series and the weight of the series: on the cells `D-` σ₀ 3.0 brings `M11.same` of grade 1 to 0.9135 and leaves grade 5 at 0.5725, σ₀ 2.0 and 4.0 are worse under both grades — under grade 5 the tasks of the trial series move with the start, and a prior of any width moves with them —, and at the size of the deciding run σ₀ 3.0 alone fails 17 checks "no worse" on twelve cells of risk alone — the error of the estimate after the 200th answer and Brier on both maths, the start of `S-adult` —, as this exploration found at 300 learners; with the step after the series no set holds every constraint either (measurements 5 and 7);
  3. the guessing of `predicate` did not go into the exploration: no pinned package has a check `predicate` — `grade-3-math` 0.1.0 and `olympiad-math` 0.1.1 have `choice`, `number`, `fraction` and sheets, `english-essays` 0.1.0 has `choice`, `rubric` and sheets (the packages' `server.json`) —, and the synthetic `S-predicate` runs as a cell "without a graph", whose checks are "neither met nor failed". The number stays in Д5 until a module with `predicate`.
- **The judgement** — deciding runs at 1,000 working learners per cell, what a deciding run weighed at that tag of `sim`, on `engine` and `learner-model` v0.1.4 of the base and on the candidates' commits, 41–44 minutes each; the report is `sim`'s `judgements/model-1/` (`main` 0364b40, read 2026-11-10):

  | Candidate | Estimate | `forgets` | Failed "no worse" |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | `g10` | 0.025 [0.011; 0.037] | 0.195 [0.098; 0.294] | 4: `M1.rms.200` on G3-drop, G8 and S-scale1.5 of the olympiad one and `M3.settle` on F-break |
  | `halve035` | 0.014 [0.002; 0.027] | 0.117 [0.031; 0.213] | 3: on the edge of the tolerance — `E3.late` on F-growth5, 0.04967 at 0.049, and `M1.rms.200` on A-unpractised, 0.03116 at 0.03076 —, and `M3.settle` on F-break — 19.42 at 18.11, 7 % past the tolerance |
  | `forget-set` | 0.033 [0.020; 0.046] | 0.277 [0.184; 0.373] | 8: the same as `g10`'s, `E1.corridor` on G2, G2-fading and S-adult and `M1.rms.200` on F-none of the olympiad one |

  What the judgement showed beyond the exploration:
  - the gain in `forgets` was confirmed, but 1.7–2.7 times smaller than at the rough look: at 300 learners — 0.40, 0.32 and 0.46;
  - the common failure of the three is the catching up after a break, `M3.settle` on F-break: with the new numbers the model catches up with the truth after a break for longer. The exploration saw it only for the set, and for `g10`, of `M1.rms.200` on the olympiad one, only G3-drop, without G8 and S-scale1.5: at 300 learners the resolution of "no worse" is wider, and these failures passed for noise. Why the catching up is slower, the judgement does not read;
  - there was no confirmation, and the held-out learners `held-out-model-1` stay untouched.

— *Why so:* both chosen numbers are constants of the model's forgetting, taken from systems for flashcards, and the exploration showed that the model catches the forgetting of a skill better when the stability grows more slowly and a wrong answer cuts it more (Д6). Both numbers hardly touch the other groups, and the constraints they violate are on the edge of the tolerance, and the base 0.35 has only one. The set — because both numbers move one group in one direction, and the judgement must see whether they add up.

— *Why an exit, not a tolerance:* the tolerances "no worse" were recorded before the first candidate, and they may not be shifted for a candidate.

— *What the exploration does not decide:* a rough look decides nothing. At 300 learners no variant holds all the constraints "no worse", and the deciding run did not hold them either: its resolution is narrower, and the author's tolerance is the same. The exploration ran on `engine` `v0.1.0` and the judgement on `v0.1.4`, and the directions stayed: the edit of `engine` between them is a lesson for a skill from the map, not forgetting.

— *Rejected:*
- a candidate for each group — for σ₀ 3.0 and ρ 1/4 the best groups are paid for by others and by broken constraints, and the criterion judges the overall score;
- more than three candidates — the plan of the epic holds the first judgement to three;
- a run at 1,000 learners for the three best — about 1 h 40 min per variant on a free machine and more on a busy one, and it does not change the choice: the intervals of the overall score of the three are already above zero.

— *Consequence:* nothing changes in the code, the tests and the documents of `main`: the three candidates were draft pull requests, closed with a reason, and only in them did the base of the division of τ, which stands in the formula `stabilityAfter`, become a named constant. — `run/DEBT.md`, Д5–Д7.

## The plan of E4

**R25. Epic E4 is format 0.4 in the model. First the move to the latest tag of `spec` and the gate on the latest tag of `sim`, then the value `time` of `CheckKind` and the field of the leading question in one pull request through the gate and in one release, then a faster `Readout.Task`, then the result. The executor takes every task of the epic through to the merge into `main` himself.**
- **What was found in planning** (`gh release list`, clones of `spec` and `sim` on `main`, read 2026-11-11):
  - the latest release of `spec` is v0.14.0, and `main` stands on it. `spec`'s `COORDINATION.md` sets the order of format 0.4: a release of `learner-model` with `time` and the field of the leading question, then `sim` reads them and releases a tag, and only then `modules` releases v0.2.1; `api` takes the same release. The tag also brings `learner-model` a rule of the export: `api` puts the `.proto` of the version that computed into the export, so the comments of the `.proto` must explain each field without code, and the release must name the `.proto` file a file of the export;
  - the latest tag of `sim` is v0.1.5, commit `dd0391958105195cb21193db1c2b9fbc71b2fe1e`: `engine` and `learner-model` v0.1.4, `modules` v0.2.0 of format 0.4, where there is neither `time` nor leading questions. So the code of format 0.4 does not move the gate's numbers. `sim`'s epic «Стенд за библиотеками» is under way: it will release a tag on `engine` v0.1.5, and its reading of format 0.4 waits for the release from here (`sim` on `main` d001515);
  - the code of format 0.4 is the value `CHECK_KIND_TIME` in `common.proto`, the field in `evidence.proto` after `technique_on_request`, the types, the codec, the check kind in `knownChecks` (`step.go`), which the step, the trial series and `Readout.Task` call, and the rule `helped`. `time` already has no guessing: only `choice` gives it (`model.go`). Whether to raise `EvidenceKind` to 2 — the kind changes when a field is added — is decided in planning the task of the code.
- **What is decided:**
  1. E4 is the next epic, and the second round of candidates, E5, comes after it;
  2. the tasks are the move to `spec` v0.14.0, the gate on the latest tag of `sim`, `time` and the leading question in one release, a faster `Readout.Task` and the result, in this order. The gate takes the tag of `sim` that is the latest when its task runs; if `sim` releases a new one before the merge of the release of format 0.4, the gate is raised to it by a repeat of the gate's task — by its own pull request on the code of `main` —, so that a red gate does not confuse new bands with new code. The comments of the `.proto` for the export go into the release of format 0.4: it edits the `.proto`, and the schema comes out with a release;
  3. the executor himself commits, opens the pull request, waits for green CI and merges each task of E4 into `main` — a departure from the rule that the author commits after review.

— *Why so:* `spec`'s order puts this release before `sim` and `modules` v0.2.1, and `sim` and `api` wait for it. A second round without an answer to why the model takes longer to catch up with the truth after a break would repeat the first, which ended with an exit on it. The move goes first, because the order and the rule of the export come with the tag, and the repository moves to a new tag by a task of its own. The gate goes before the code, as in E3: the code must pass the gate on the pin on which `sim` will read `time`. A faster `Readout.Task` is a change of the model of its own, through the gate, so it goes after the release that `sim`, `modules` and `api` wait for.

— *Rejected:*
- E5 first — `sim` and `modules` would wait for the release of format 0.4, and the second round would start without an answer on the catching up after a break;
- the code before the move and the gate — the release would pass the gate on the bands of `sim` v0.1.2 with `engine` v0.1.0, not on the pin where `sim` will read it;
- the move and the gate as one task — each has its own check and its own risk: the move releases a patch with the same code, and the gate may turn red through no fault of the code.

— *Consequence:* RUN.md, `run/EPICS.md`, `run/QUESTIONS.md`, `run/DEBT.md`; CLAUDE.md, README.md.

## The `spec` tag v0.14.0

**R26. `learner-model` stands on `spec` v0.14.0, the latest release. The tag recorded two rows «в реестр spec» of this repository — on its part of the release of format 0.4 and on the tag of `sim` for the gates —, and the rows are closed, and beyond them it brings the order of the release of format 0.4 and the format of the export into which `api` puts this library's `.proto`. The move changes neither the code nor `.devcontainer/`: the output of the recipe `new-repo` is the same byte for byte, so the merge gives no release.**
- **What the tag brings `learner-model`** (`git -C spec diff v0.12.0 v0.14.0`, read 2026-11-11; `spec`'s RUN.md and `COORDINATION.md`):
  - the order of format 0.4, `spec`'s answer to a row of `sim`: the harness reads format 0.4 before the modules. A release of `learner-model` with `time` of `CheckKind` and the field of the leading question, in one release through the gate of `sim`; then `sim` — the translation of `time` and the leading question in the learner's truth on this release, and a tag; then `modules` — v0.2.1 with them. `api` takes the same release before the packages with `time`. This is the task of the code of E4;
  - the answer on this repository's part of format 0.4: the move of `learner-model` to a tag does not close it, it is open until the release with the field and `time`, and `api` waits for this release. `spec` recorded the row with the answer, so it leaves here, and that the part is open until the release is held by the debt of format 0.4;
  - the answer on the tag of `sim` for the gates: `sim` `v0.1.3` stood on `engine` and `learner-model` v0.1.4, and the first 100 learners of each cell gave the same bytes as the run of the base of the first exploration, so the paths and bands stayed. It tells the gates of the libraries to move to `sim` v0.1.3 — the gate's task takes the latest tag, v0.1.5, which is newer than it. `spec` recorded the row with the answer, and it leaves;
  - the format of the export, [the part `export/`](../spec/export/README.md): a ZIP of JSON files, where `api` writes the evidence log and the state as the JSON of their messages `novatrail.learnermodel.v1` by protobuf's mapping to JSON with the field names as in the `.proto`, and beside them, in `api/proto/`, the `common.proto`, `evidence.proto` and `state.proto` of the version of `learner-model` that computed, from its release file, which `release-verify` accepted; the rule of `export.proto` requires them in every export with a profile. `spec` tells `learner-model` to keep the comments of the `.proto` such that the reader of an export understands each field without code, and to name in the release the `.proto` file a file that `api` puts into the export. In the schema's archive the files lie under `learner-model-proto-<version>/proto/novatrail/learnermodel/v1/`, and in the export under `api/proto/novatrail/learnermodel/v1/` (the reading vectors of the part `export/`): `api` strips the top folder and `proto/` and puts the files under `api/proto/`, by the package's path;
  - `spec`'s open format of the export is the part `export/`, and what the platform keeps as protobuf bytes goes out as JSON of the same value with its own `.proto`. It has no new rule for the library beyond the comments of the `.proto`;
  - the trap `twelve-hours-off` for the kind `time` in the format's example `olympiad-math`: a time after noon written as the clock face shows it — the hours counted from noon anew. The model reads the found traps by ID, whatever the kind of the check ([architecture.md](architecture.md)), so the trap needs no new code, but the test of the code of format 0.4 with a slot `time` may take it as an example;
  - the rest between the tags — `spec`'s decisions on the legal texts and consents, the age of digital consent, the operator's bucket, the reports of a problem, self-hosting and the platform's operator, the parts `legal/` and `consent-age/` of `spec`'s Go module, and the edits of `spec`'s documents on the same — does not concern `learner-model`: the library stores nothing and sees no learner's data but what `api` passes to it. The component's card in `spec`, RULES.md, the template `repo-template/`, `spec`'s `.devcontainer/` and the recipe `new-repo` did not change; the documents check got the document `dpia` added.
- **What is changed here:**
  - `spec/` — at v0.14.0, commit c318cb4; `gh release verify v0.14.0 -R altnovatrail/spec` — "Release v0.14.0 verified!", the release is immutable and the latest (`gh release view` and `gh release list`, 2026-11-11). The tag is in CLAUDE.md, README.md and at the top of RUN.md;
  - `.devcontainer/` — the output of `just new-repo-draft learner-model go 3.8 v0.14.0` in a clone of `spec` in a temporary folder matched it byte for byte (`diff -r`, 2026-11-11): there is no edit;
  - [architecture.md](architecture.md) — the order of format 0.4 and its task of code; the format of the export is decided, and the `.proto` go into it;
  - RUN.md and `run/` — the two rows are closed; the debt of format 0.4 has the tag and the order.

— *Why now:* E4 starts with the move: the order of format 0.4 and the rule of the export are only in v0.14.0, and the epic's tasks refer to them. The repository moves to a new tag by a task of its own and records the move as a decision.

— *Why the comments of the `.proto` are not here:* the schema is in the release's `paths`, so an edit here would give a new version of the model only for the comments, before the release with the field and `time` — and `api`'s cache mark is the model's version, so `api`, taking it, would recompute every learner's cache without any change in the numbers. The task of the code edits the schema anyway and releases both in one release.

— *Rejected:*
- v0.13.0 — the order of format 0.4 is not in it yet, and a move takes the latest release;
- keep the row on this repository's part of format 0.4 open until its release — a row «в реестр spec» leaves when `spec` has recorded it; that the part is not taken yet is held by the debt of format 0.4, and the release tells of itself by a new row;
- rebuild the container — `.devcontainer/` is the same, and so is the image.

— *Consequence:* the merge does not touch the release's `paths`, so it gives no release. `spec`'s `COORDINATION.md` will see the tag by the submodule. — `spec/`; CLAUDE.md, README.md, RUN.md, `run/QUESTIONS.md`, `run/DEBT.md`; [architecture.md](architecture.md).
